# Map of every area (20 mappers)

## Move grading and verdict wording (grades, cost words, the verdict store)
The fault band itself is shared: win% lost at 5/10/20, in accuracyService.gradeMove/bandForWinPctLost. Everything around it is split. There are two label vocabularies (MoveClassification and moveRating.MoveQuality, plus a third MoveQuality in tacticTypes). Mate rules exist in three places: gradeMove, classifyCpLoss and classifyMoveFull. A single Learn move is graded up to four times from different engine reads (gradePlayedMove, detectSlip, backwardLook, inaccuracyCall). There are also five side graders: tacticClassifier, classifyEvalSwing (handed to the LLM), liveCoachTriggers, a dead gradeGuess, and an engine-free GuessTheMove grader.

The verdict store is the right shape for G7 but is half-wired. Only Learn and Review write to it, and only batch analysis reads it. It keeps faults only. One plausible path stores a walk-into-mate as 'fine', which then overrides Review's blunder.

The rulebook asks for Miss, Best, Excellent and the "only move or turns the game" Great; none of these is computed. The 'walked-into-tactic' and 'missed-forcing-win' reasons are dead because their inputs are hard-coded to 0.

The wording is copied about seven times (three verdict ladders in groundedAnswer, QUALITY_CLAUSE, CLASSIFICATION_LINES, costWords vs cpToWords, evalPhrase). It speaks numbers (V8), talks about "the engine" and "I" (V1), and drops the better move's squares in Review and Learn (S10).

For the one brain: grading should be ONE computer producing ONE stored record per move, holding grade, reason class, cost-kind, outcome-band crossing, better move and refutation line, with their squares. That record would fill the TEMPTING-CHOICE and THE-LINE roles and feed the student record. Each surface's speaking floor would move into the one decider.
MISSING: ONLY-MOVE GRADE (G3 Great, half 1): best move with every alternative losing the evaluation band (gap best vs 2nd in win%, not cp). moveReason.only-move uses gap12 >= 150cp, Learn only. Example: defending a mating attack with the one interposition. Every few games. IDENTIFY/PREVENT.; STATE-CROSSING GRADE (G3 Great, half 2 + the cost word G4/V8 wants): which outcome band a move moved the game between (lost/worse/equal/better/winning), e.g. 'that turned an equal game into a lost one'. No computer outputs the band pair; costWords speaks pawns. Every graded fault. CONSEQUENCE.; MISS (G5): the opponent's previous move was a fault AND the student's reply gave back the gain (opponent winPctLost >= band, student's reply returns >= half of it). Example: they hang a knight, you don't take. 1 in 3 amateur games. RECOGNIZE.; BEST vs EXCELLENT split in the stored grade (G4): win% lost 0 vs < 2. Code computes it in moveRating but drops it.; ESCAPE / LUCKY: the student's fault went unpunished because the opponent missed it (the mirror of Miss), so the coach can teach the punishment the opponent missed. Common below 1500. PREVENT/CONSEQUENCE.; COST BY KIND: what the move gave up as a named thing (material, castling rights, the bishop pair, a tempo, pawn structure, king cover, the initiative), not centipawns. Nothing turns a fault into its kind except moveReason's hung-piece. Every fault. CONSEQUENCE.; WIN->DRAW / DRAW->LOSS in a non-tablebase position (theoretical drawing technique missed, e.g. wrong-colour bishop, rook pawn). Only the endgame tablebase verdict has it. Rare (1 in 50 games). KNOWLEDGE.; UNSOUND BUT PRACTICAL SACRIFICE (?!/!?): a sacrifice the engine refutes deep but that is hard to meet at the student's level (refutation is an only-move or far away). verifySacrificeDeep exists but only gates Brilliant. Rare. STRATEGIZE.; GRADE CONFIDENCE: does the band hold across depths (shallow vs deep read disagree), so the coach doesn't call a horizon artifact a blunder. Code patches individual cases (bestMoveEqualsPlayed, MATE_HORIZON) but nothing outputs it. Every game somewhere. Internal (protects every verb).; REPEATED-FAULT JOIN at grade time: this fault matches the same reason class / weakness tag as a past game ('third time hanging a piece on the f-file'). The spine has the record; no grader hands it to the verdict. Frequent. RECOGNIZE (habit).; TIME-WASTE / TEMPO GRADE in the opening: a move that loses no eval yet moves a piece twice or makes an unnecessary pawn move (V19c says 'always taught'). Graders all say 'good' (win% flat). Very common below 1200. KNOWLEDGE/PLAN.; FAULT IN A DECIDED POSITION, TOLD AS TECHNIQUE: in a won position, the move that prolongs the win or allows counterplay (mover still winning, win% flat, but distance-to-conversion grew). Graded no fault today. Common in won games. STRATEGIZE.; OPPONENT-SIDE GRADE in Learn (their slip as the student's chance), same grader as the student's moves. Review has it via studentAnswer; Learn's gradePlayedMove grades only the student. Every game. RECOGNIZE.; GRADE OF THE BOOK MOVE that is a known population error (book but dubious at the student's band, e.g. 3...Bg4? in the gem example). The book override erases it. Opening, frequent. RECOGNIZE (traps).

## Why-a-move / better-move reasons (explainBestMoveGrounded, deliberation, moveInsight, betterMoveReason, compareTwoMoves, computeMoveWhy and siblings)
This area answers one chess question: "why is this move good, and why is the other one worse?" About 20 separate computers answer it, and they never share a structured fact. Six answer "why the better move is better": explainBestMoveGrounded, betterMoveReason, deliberation.moveWhy, compareTwoMoves, explainMoveOrder and assembleCompareMovesAnswer. About nine answer "what this move does": computeMoveWhyDetail, groundedMoveWhy, quietPurposePhrase, describeMoveMerit, strategicWhyLed, studentMovePoint/quietMovePoint, buildReviewMoveTeaching and assembleMovePurpose. Five pick the "tempting choice": deliberation alternatives, buildRejectedTempting, tacticalRead.pickTempting, refutedAlternative and assembleAlternativesAnswer. Three explain move order: moveOrder.ts, checksFirst and explainMoveOrder.

Review alone can attach four different reasons to one flagged move, and Learn, Play and chat each use a different head computer. This breaks F2, D1 and D7.

For the one thinking chain (F0c/F18) this area should be REASON, TEMPTING CHOICE + why it fails, and THE LINE. deliberation already has that shape: it lists the candidates, rules each out with a proof, then gives the verdict with its reason and line. But it is turned off in the opening, it weighs only 3 candidates, and its clause reaches positionFacts as text alone, with no squares or lines.

Most reasoners return a bare string. That includes explainBestMoveGrounded (about 15 call sites), betterMoveReason (which throws away the square whyBetter computed), moveWhy, groundedMoveWhy and studentMovePoint. So arrows have to be re-read from the prose (S10).

Fix direction: one typed reason fact {role, kind, squares, lineUci, stakes} that every surface reads. compareTwoMoves' ablation method should become the engine for "why", with the missing kinds added (king safety, structure, activity, prevention).
MISSING: ONE STRUCTURED REASON FACT: no type {role, kind, squares, lineUci, stakes, seat} that every why-computer fills. Every reasoner returns prose, so the F0c composer and S10 arrows have nothing to read.; REMOVE-THE-DEFENDER / DEFLECTION / DECOY / OVERLOAD as the best move's REASON: explainBestMoveGrounded names only fork, pin, check, material or quiet purpose. Example: Rxe8+ deflects the queen off the d8 guard. Occurs in about 1 of 10 tactical best moves. Verbs: recognize, consequence.; INTERFERENCE, CLEARANCE, X-RAY DEFENCE and DESPERADO as reasons: none of the why computers name them. Rare (about 1 in 200 positions). Verb: recognize.; PROPHYLAXIS REASON for the student's best move ('it stops …Nd4 before they play it'): moveIntent.stopReason exists only for the opponent's seat and never feeds moveWhy or explainBestMoveGrounded. Example: h3 against …Bg4 pressure. Common in quiet middlegames. Verb: prevent.; THREAT OF A THREAT / 'prepares X' for the student's best move: threatMadeWhy sees only immediate attacks. Example: Rad1 preparing e5. Very common. Verb: plan.; compareTwoMoves ablation kinds marked '(later)' and never built: king safety (remove the attacker), structure (doubled or isolated pawn created), piece activity, tempo count, development lead. Example: Bxf6 gxf6 versus Be3 (shattered king shelter). Verb: consequence.; ONLY-MOVE explanation: why EVERY alternative loses, not just the best runner-up. criticalityScan knows it is an only move, but no computer composes 'all three other moves drop the e5 pawn'. About 1 in 30 middlegame positions. Verb: consequence.; ENDGAME MOVE REASONS: takes the opposition, enters or stays in the square of the pawn, cuts the king off along a file, shoulders the king, triangulates, the rook behind the passed pawn. No why-computer emits these as a move's reason. Example: Kd5 taking the opposition in K+P vs K. Common in endgames. Verb: knowledge.; TRADE-WHEN-AHEAD reason ('trade pieces, not pawns'): deliberation has only the queen trade while ahead on space or attack (deliberation.ts:186). Example: offering a rook trade at +3. Verb: strategize.; COLOUR-COMPLEX reason: a bishop trade that leaves the dark squares weak, or the good-vs-bad bishop as the why of a move. Example: …Bxf3 handing over the light squares. Verb: strategize.; PAWN-CHAIN-BASE reason: 'attacks the chain at its base' for a break move (c5 versus d4–e5 in the French). Concept corpus only, never computed as a move reason. Verb: plan.; RESTRICTION reason: a move that takes the flight squares of one specific enemy piece ('b4 takes c5 and a5 from the knight'). Example: g4 against Nf5. Verb: prevent.; WHICH-PIECE / WHICH-ROOK reason: the same square reached by two pieces (Rfe1 vs Rae1, Nbd2 vs Nfd2) and why one is better. candidateCompareRead only compares squares for the same piece. Verb: plan.; COUNT-THE-DEFENDERS as a spoken reason when a capture fails or works ('two attackers, two defenders — taking loses a piece'): SEE is computed but never phrased as the counting method on a reason. Verb: knowledge.; TRANSITION reason: a capture or trade that wins because the resulting pawn ending or minor-piece ending is won (or drawn). Example: Rxd7 Rxd7 Kxd7 into a won K+P ending. Rare (about 1 in 300 games) but decisive. Verb: consequence.; IN-BETWEEN MOVE as the best move's reason, outside the autopilot-recapture case (moveInsight.autopilotRecapture :642 covers only an immediate recapture). Example: a zwischenzug check before taking back. Verb: recognize.
BROKEN: DEAD: computeMoveWhy (src/services/moveWhy.ts:171) has no production caller; only computeMoveWhyDetail is used.; DEAD: computeWhyBestMove (src/services/whyBestMove.ts:63) has no production caller; both surfaces use computeWhyBestMoveDetail.; DEAD: candidateCompareClause (src/services/tacticalRead.ts:604) has no caller.; DEAD: materialWhiteMinusBlack and PIECE_WORD in src/services/moveComparison.ts:76-79 are reachable only through __test (:267).; SQUARES DROPPED: whyBetter returns {why, square, own} (inaccuracyCall.ts:316,412), but betterMoveFact (:155) keeps only why/own. Every betterMoveReason surface loses the square it computed (S10).; SQUARES DROPPED: leadingFundamentals has f.squares (moveFundamentals.ts ~1144), but strategicWhyLed/SelfContained/Imperative (:1155-1182) return strings only.; NO SQUARES: explainBestMoveGrounded (groundedAnswer.ts:2448) returns a string at about 15 call sites, so arrows for the best move and the punishing capture must be re-derived from the prose.; DELIBERATION UNARROWED: positionFacts pushes the deliberation clause with only {kind, rank, text, stakes} (positionFacts.ts:1339-1342). The candidates it names and the best line get no squares or lines. bestLine is kept as text only (deliberation.ts:206-207), and the UCI line is dropped.; PROSE-READ DECISION: positionFacts decides verdictSpeaks by regex on the text `/ The move is /` (positionFacts.ts ~1001). This is a decision read off prose instead of a structured flag.; OPENING GAP vs F02/V19/F18(2): buildDeliberation is skipped in the opening phase (positionFacts.ts:479, `!openingPhase`). The tempting-choice weighing never runs on the moves the rulebook ranks highest.; CAPS (V14/S10): deliberation DEFAULT_MAX=3 (deliberation.ts:54); assembleAlternativesAnswer `lines.slice(1, 4)` (groundedAnswer.ts ~2358); assembleEngineReasoning maxFollowUps default 2 (:3221); assembleCompareMovesAnswer pvSans(..., 4) (groundedAnswer.ts ~7300); buildDrillBetterLine maxPlies=6 (learnMoveTeaching.ts:77); squaresNamedIn cap=4 (moveWhy.ts:179) truncates highlights.; V8 NUMBERS: assembleCompareMovesAnswer speaks digits: `about ${pawns} points worse` with pawns = (gap/100).toFixed(1) (groundedAnswer.ts:7285-7290).; RECAPTURE MISREAD RISK: strongChoice calls moveWhy(fenBefore, best.san, mover, null) (strongChoice.ts:32). opponentLastSan is the only recapture guard in materialWhy (deliberation.ts:41), so a strong move that recaptures can be said as 'wins the X'.; RECAPTURE MISREAD RISK: explainBestMoveGrounded gets prevCapture=null from almost every caller (whyBestMove.ts:104, coachApi.ts:3499,5671, learnMoveTeaching.ts:43, coachFeatureService.ts:2582,2593, assembleAlternativesAnswer). Only groundedAnswer.ts:1799 passes it, so the 'takes back' fix (:2489-2491) is mostly unreachable. Most callers also pass lines=null, so the ledger proof is skipped.; S7 CONFLICT: the Play/Openings Why answer leads with the corpus note (whyBestMove.ts:81 positionTeachingWhy). S7 allows library notes only in teach-me-X, chat, tactics drills and endgame lessons.; V4 FILLER: computeMoveWhyDetail emits generic lines ('The rook swings to X, eyeing the file', 'Castling gets the king to safety…', moveWhy.ts:113,160). groundedMoveWhy.moveTypeFloor is deliberately never-empty filler ('developing the knight toward the centre', groundedMoveWhy.ts:32).; TWO COMPARE ENGINES: chat's assembleCompareMovesAnswer does not use compareTwoMoves. compareTwoMoves can prove only material and passed-pawn; its header lists tactic, king safety and structure as '(later)' (moveComparison.ts:16), and they were never built.; REVIEW MULTI-REASONER: one flagged move can draw its reason from betterMoveReason (coachFeatureService:1015), explainMoveOrder (:1022), explainBestMoveGrounded (:2582/2593), compareTwoMoves on a fresh 6-ply computePvLine (:4003), and the re-read betterMoveReason (:4094,4141). turningPoints chains betterMoveReason then moveWhy (turningPoints.ts:257-262). Different engine reads can give different reasons (D1, D7).

## Turning points, criticality, importance, thesis/recap producers
This area is the brain's "is this moment worth stopping for, and what was the game about" layer. It is fragmented. There are five separate gap computers (scanCriticality, readCriticalMoment, speedRunReads.choiceGap, positionCharacter.sharpGap, criticality.ts gap12) and five decided-game gates that disagree (600cp, WDL 900, 75 win-%, 300cp, a raw material count). Four turning-point pickers run on Review: selectTurningPoints (student, win-%), turningPointCandidates (either side, cp), buildCriticalMomentQuestion (fan count), and the narrative recap (the first three mistakes in game order). On top of them sit three more key-moment definitions (the review "critical" flag at |swing|≥150, KeyMomentNav, statusBandChange). Review speaks about "the moment" through 8 producers with no shared order or say-once.

The strongest computers for the one chain are:
- readCriticalMoment as TEMPTING CHOICE + why it fails, because its discard proofs come from each line's own engine line.
- selectTurningPoints as HABIT, because it counts the cause across the game.
- computeImportance as the gate. It decides when a moment speaks, so it fills no role itself.
- selectTeaching as GOAL/thesis. But its thesis cannot fire in phase narration (no evals are passed), and Review runs it twice with different inputs.

Dead or dropped:
- onlyMoveSequence (no callers).
- the computeCriticality score (no reader).
- the turning-point question chips (built, never asked).

Almost nothing here carries moves or squares. Proofs, the "why", what the move allowed, the hinge and the thesis are all prose, so S10 arrows on the lines these computers describe are impossible today. The fix: each fact returns its line in UCI along with its text.
MISSING: POINT OF NO RETURN: the last ply where the game could still be saved/held (after it, best play never recovers the stake) - distinct from the biggest swing. Example: drifting from +0.3 to -1 over 4 moves then -4; the GM names the move after which it was gone. Every lost game. Verb: consequence.; FIRST CAUSE vs BIGGEST SWING: the earlier quiet move that made the later blunder possible (prophylaxis one move earlier). causalChain exists but nothing labels 'the real mistake was a move earlier'. Common in amateur losses. Verb: prevent.; GAME SHAPE of the eval trajectory: single collapse, slow drift, comeback, seesaw, lead changes count. Every game. Verb: strategize.; CONVERSION SLIP: a decided-same-side swing (+8 to +3, or WDL win dropping) - every turning-point picker explicitly skips decided games, so 'you let a won game get complicated / stopped converting' is never computed. Frequent in 800-1300 games. Verb: strategize.; OPPONENT'S MISSED CHANCE / unpunished blunder pair: their blunder followed by the student not taking it (and how long the tactic sat on the board for both sides). Partly turningCause 'missed' but no mutual-blindness duration. Frequent at low ratings. Verb: recognize.; OPPONENT ONLY-MOVE moments: positions where the OPPONENT faced an only move (scanCriticalMoments scans student plies only) - 'you set them a problem and they solved it / failed it'. Verb: plan.; PRACTICAL DIFFICULTY: an objectively level position where one side faces a run of only-moves ahead (count of only-moves along the PV per side) - 'equal on paper, hard to play for you'. Verb: strategize.; MISSED DEFENSIVE RESOURCE at a lost turning point: stalemate trick, perpetual check, fortress, opposite bishops - the last drawing chance. Rare (1 in 50 games) but high value. Verb: knowledge.; SWINDLE MOVE: in a lost position, the move setting the most practical traps (largest count of natural opponent replies that blunder). Verb: plan.; TRADE-INTO-ENDGAME decision: the simplification that converted (or threw) the game - 'you traded into a lost pawn ending' as a computed turning point. Verb: consequence.; CLEAN-WIN THESIS: thesis is 'none' when no swing; a won game with no blunder has no computed 'how you won' (plan/structure/piece that decided it). Verb: strategize.; WHOSE TURNING POINT: thesis/turningPointCandidates don't say whether the game turned on the student's move or the opponent's ('they gave it to you' vs 'you threw it'). Verb: identify.; TIME AND CRITICALITY: clock data is imported (gameImportUtils, chesscomGamesService) but nothing joins time spent with criticality ('you spent 3 seconds on the only-move position, 2 minutes on a recapture'). Common online. Verb: prevent.; CRITICAL-MOMENT RECOGNITION SIGNALS: what on the board should have told the student this was critical (a check, a capture, a loose piece, an open king file, tension) - the coach says 'slow down' but not 'here is how you could have known'. Verb: recognize.; CROSS-GAME TURNING PATTERN: 'your last three losses all turned in the same way' (same cause id across games) - causeCount is per game only (turningPoints.ts:227). Verb: recognize.; OPENING-INDUCED DISADVANTAGE: the first ply the eval left balance in the opening, joined to theory departure ('you left book on move 6 and were worse by move 9'). Verb: knowledge.

Turning-point + thesis + recap = 8 producers speaking about 'the moment' on Review alone (selectTurningPoints, buildCriticalMomentQuestion, buildTurningPointQuestion reveal, renderThesis, computeTurningPointHinge, generateNarrativeSummary, renderFundamentalsRecap, phaseScopedReviewSummary) with no shared say-once or ordering.; ## Threats: detection, prevention, answers (detectNewThreat, computeMustDefend, threatAnswer, latentDanger, danger lanes)
Threats are the strongest "one vocabulary" candidate and today speak at least SIX vocabularies. Detection: detectNewThreat (null-move: mate-in-1 / royal-or-major fork / SEE capture), computeMustDefend (null-move + findHangingPieces + legal SEE), assembleThreatAnswer (chat, static SEE only), deliberation.threatMadeWhy, Learn's own inline lanes in CoachTeachPage (tacticsDetector immediate/PV "soon", hang, hit-by-smaller, king-pawn), Play's scanUpcomingTactics+isCriticalThreat (computed, then silenced). Answers: threatAnswer (Learn), describeThreatPrevention (Review), deliberation.threatAnswerWhy, speedRunReads.takeTheSting, falseAlarm, moveInsight.positionAsk's "is the threat real" branch, thinkingAnswerDangerStep (move-it only). Each surface therefore names and answers a different set of threats. The record splits too: the tag 'missed-opponents-threat' is decided by computeMustDefend in weaknessSpine but by detectNewThreat in moveInsight, which is the discovery/discovered_attack disease again. For the one chain: detection = OBSTACLE plus "what their move changed" (F18 step 1); threatAnswer/describeThreatPrevention = REMOVE IT; falseAlarm/bluff/takeTheSting = the TEMPTING reaction and why it is wrong; describeThreatRecognition/forkAlignmentClause/latentDanger = HABIT. The merge should be ONE threat fact (kind, attacker square, victim squares, guards, stakes) with ONE answer classifier, carrying from/to so S10 arrows come from the fact. Today the pin, PV "soon" and king-pawn warnings in Learn draw no arrow, and Review scrapes the SAN back out of its own prose. The biggest missing fact is that detectNewThreat ignores forks of minor pieces and pawn-push forks, which are the most common amateur threat. Another is that chat can say "they have no immediate threat" while a mate-in-1 is standing.
MISSING: MINOR-PIECE AND PAWN-FORK THREATS: detectNewThreat counts only K/Q/R as fork victims (groundedAnswer.ts:6928-6933). The commonest amateur threat, a pawn push forking two minors (e4-e5 vs Nf6+Bd6, …d5 vs Bc4+Ne4), is not a threat to any lane. forkTrick catches it only after a capture-recapture. Example: 1.e4 e5 2.Nf3 Nc6 3.Bc4 Nf6 4.Nc3?! Nxe4 5.Nxe4 d5. Frequent (several per 100 club games). Serves IDENTIFY/PREVENT.; DISCOVERED ATTACK IN WAITING AGAINST THE STUDENT: an enemy piece standing in front of its own slider aimed at the student's king/queen is skipped on purpose by latentDanger.ts:139-141 ('the opponent's discovery, a different concern'), and nothing else computes it. Example: Black Bd6 in front of Qd8 on the d-file toward White's Qd1 after the d-pawns are traded. Occasional (1-3% of middlegame plies). PREVENT.; THREATS TWO MOVES OUT FOR SLIDERS AND MATE: latentFork is knights-only (latentFork.ts:26-32) and detectNewThreat is one null-move ply. Nothing sees a Greek-gift set-up (Bd3+Qc2 battery threatening Bxh7+), a mate-in-2 threat, or a queen-and-bishop battery forming. Example: French Advance, White Bd3, Qc2, Ng5 ideas. Rare-to-occasional, decisive when it happens. PREVENT/RECOGNIZE.; COUNTER-THREAT AS THE ANSWER (zwischenzug / meet a threat with a bigger one): threatAnswer has no 'counter' kind (threatAnswer.ts:22) and returns null or 'wait'. describeThreatPrevention has no such case. The How-to-Think answer step teaches only 'move it'. Example: their knight hits your queen, and you answer Bxf7+ first. Occasional. STRATEGIZE/CONSEQUENCE.; DOUBLE THREAT ⇒ CANNOT PARRY BOTH: no computer says 'two threats at once — you can only meet one; pick which to give up', or ranks which threat to answer. Example: queen fork of rook and loose bishop with a mate threat behind it. Occasional. CONSEQUENCE/knowledge nugget.; TRAPPED-PIECE THREAT: a pawn push or quiet move that will leave a student piece with no safe square (bishop on g3 after h4-h5 ideas, knight on a5/h5). findTrappedPiece runs only after the move, on the current board (reviewTeachingPoints.ts:789); it is not a threat lane. Example: Bc8-g4 then h3, g4. Occasional. PREVENT.; PROMOTION / PASSED-PAWN RACE THREAT FROM THE OPPONENT: a passer that queens unless stopped now (the square-of-the-pawn nugget), as a threat warning. planRace covers same-kind races only and is not a threat lane. Example: K+P endings, an outside passer on a6. Common in endings. IDENTIFY/knowledge.; BACK-RANK THREAT AS A STANDING DANGER WITH ITS CURE (luft): tacticsDetector.findBackRankWeakness exists (tacticsDetector.ts:384) but no threat lane says 'your back rank is weak — make luft before they double rooks'. Example: Rd8+ ideas when f2/g2/h2 are all at home and the d-file is open. Common. PREVENT/HABIT.; OVERLOADED DEFENDER THREAT: findOverloadedPieces exists in tacticsDetector (:690) only as a current tactic, never as 'your queen is guarding two things — they will hit one'. Occasional. IDENTIFY.; POSITIONAL THREATS (their plan, not a capture): …f5 break coming, knight heading to the d4 outpost, a minority attack, …h5-h4 vs a fianchetto. opponentIntent is only PV move pairs (opponentIntent.ts:56). Common. PREVENT/STRATEGIZE.; PERPETUAL-CHECK / DRAW RESOURCE THREAT: when the student is winning, the opponent's checking net (queen and king exposed) that forces a draw. Not computed. Rare-to-occasional, game-deciding. PREVENT.; STALEMATE TRICK THREAT FROM THE LOSING SIDE (desperado rook): stalemateTeaching covers the student's move that stalemates, not their sacrificial stalemate try. Rare. PREVENT.; 'WHAT DOES MY CANDIDATE ALLOW' BEFORE MOVING (pre-move blunder check for quiet moves): tradeDanger covers only captures that create a pin, and thinkingMoveSafetyStep / blunderCheck run after the move. Nothing tells a student before they play that a quiet move walks into a fork, a pin or a discovered check. Very common. PREVENT/HABIT.; THREAT-COUNTING NUGGET ON THE THREAT ITSELF: 'they attack it twice, you defend once — count before you relax' as part of the threat line (attacker vs defender arithmetic per threatened square). countMethodTeaching and attackerDefenderCount exist separately but are not coupled to the threat fact. Common. Knowledge (golden nugget).; X-RAY / SKEWER THREAT AGAINST THE STUDENT (a slider aiming through a valuable piece to a lesser one). latentDanger handles only the case where the more valuable piece is BEHIND (pins). Example: Bb5 skewering the Qd7 and Ra8 after the queen moves. Occasional. IDENTIFY.; THREAT PERSISTENCE / ESCALATION ACROSS MOVES ('they are piling up on f7: two attackers now, three next move'). Each lane deduplicates a threat to say it once; nothing tracks a growing attack count on one target. Occasional. RECOGNIZE/STRATEGIZE.
BROKEN: DEAD: threatCheck.buildThreatCheckQuestion (src/services/threatCheck.ts:41) has no importer anywhere in src.; DEAD: arrowEngine.computeThreatArrow (:61) and computeLeadEyeArrows (:104) have zero production callers (openingGenerator.ts:2391 says they were removed). withSideToMove's doc (arrowEngine.ts:46-50) says it returns null on an illegal flip, but the body (:51-57) never does.; COMPUTED AND DROPPED (G8.5): Play runs scanUpcomingTactics + isCriticalThreat + an isSpokenSentenceGrounded validator on every coach move (CoachGamePage.tsx:2890-2960), then speaks only if PLAY_VOLUNTEERS_COACHING, which is false (:281, :2962).; FALSE ABSENCE CLAIM: chat assembleThreatAnswer (groundedAnswer.ts:606-645) only scans static SEE captures, so with a standing mate-in-1 or fork threat it answers 'Nothing forcing — they have no immediate threat'. assembleOpponentMoveAnswer (:772) inherits this.; VOCABULARY SPLIT on one record tag: 'missed-opponents-threat' is decided by computeMustDefend (weaknessSpine.ts:219, hanging-only, net>=3) and by detectNewThreat (moveInsight.ts:745, which includes mate and fork threats). The same miss is or is not recorded depending on which path saw it.; WRONG ATTRIBUTION: computerRoles.ts:49 says the Learn 'threat' lane's computer is groundedAnswer.detectNewThreat. That lane is actually built inline in CoachTeachPage.tsx:8157-8310 from detectTactics, the PV scan, findHangingPieces and inline hit/king-pawn checks. Meanwhile Learn's falseAlarm uses detectNewThreat, so the same Learn turn can use two threat definitions.; S10: Learn threat lines for pins and other tactics ('vs:'), PV 'soon:' threats and king-pawn threats set no alertArrow. Only hang and hit draw one (CoachTeachPage.tsx:8197-8306). The 'soon' squares are regex-scraped from the description and cut with .slice(0,4) (:8245).; S10: moveInsight.theirMoveChanged names the threat their move made but never pushes its squares (moveInsight.ts:580-582). learnMoveTeaching.buildDrillThreatSpot returns a bare string (:118-129). The reviewFullData [threat] facet has no recSquares call (reviewFullData.ts:779-791). The chat threat answers return bestMoveFromTo null.; PROSE SCRAPE: Review rebuilds the threat SAN from its own sentence with threat.match(/threatening (\S+)/) (coachFeatureService.ts:2841) and builds its own null-move FEN (:2842-2845). The structured DetectedThreat was available (describeStudentThreat → detectNewThreat).; DOUBLE SAY IN REVIEW: the student's new threat is produced by both reviewFullData.ts:779 ([threat] facet) and coachFeatureService.ts:2834 (describeStudentThreat).; RESIDUE: isLoudAlarm still matches 'Again — ' (liveTacticsContext.ts:794) while openThreatLine says Again is never used (:807-810).; OVERCLAIM RISK: dangerLevel maps any piece worth >=5 (a rook) to 'decisive' → 'This one decides the game —' (liveTacticsContext.ts:788, :771).; STALE COMMENT: CoachTeachPage threat/tactic comments still cite the 'honesty contract withholds' rule that CLAUDE.md deleted for Learn (e.g. CoachTeachPage.tsx:8072-8074, :8222-8225).; SAFE-SQUARE DRIFT: thinkingAnswerDangerStep.safeAfterMove (:30) uses a SEE-free rule of thumb, while threatAnswer uses captureRead and latentFork/detectNewThreat use landingIsSafe. Three definitions of 'is this square safe'.; ~13 side-to-move flips in this area alone (listed on flipSideToMove). Only threatOut.flipSideToMove guards the illegal in-check flip; narrationAuditor.ts:85 is a same-name unguarded copy.; CAPS on the computed threat lists in the live package: threats/opportunities .slice(0,5) (liveTacticsContext.ts:105-106) and immediate .slice(0,5) (:370). Learn reads only [0], so this is probably an allowance and not a G4.5 cap, but it is unverified.; HALF-BUILT (not wired both ways): latentDanger, tradeDanger and latentFork(theirs) teach a prevention fact but have no record path, no tag and no computerRoles row. A student walking into the pin it warned about is never recorded as such.; positionFacts suppresses latentDanger and tradeDanger before move 10 (positionFacts.ts:462, :487-494). Opening pins in waiting (e.g. a knight that must stay on f6 while the bishop eyes it) are silent exactly where David's priority (F02) is.; detectTradeCreatesPin covers only the 1-ply capture. The recapture-creates-it case is an admitted TODO (latentDanger.ts:170-171).

## Tactics / pins / forks / hanging pieces detectors and the tactic vocabularies
This area is the coach's tactical vision, and it is wide but split. One static scanner, tacticsDetector.detectTactics, covers about ten motifs and reaches 34 files. Beside it sit a move-based classifier with weaker gates (tacticClassifier), two definitions of 'hanging', six pin tests, six fork tests, three discovered-attack tests (one of them called 'x-ray') and two skewer tests that disagree. Several 'is it real' gates sit on top (isRealPin, isScenicPawnPin, verifyForkOnBoard, tacticWinsMaterial). So the same board can be taught as a tactic on one surface and filed as something else in the record. That breaks the shared vocabulary the loop depends on.

Concrete defects: computeMustDefend misses a defended piece attacked by a cheaper one. A removal-of-the-guard hole is drilled with defensive-move puzzles. Live lists are cut to 5 and missed tactics to 10. Rating decides how far ahead the coach looks and when it speaks. Several computers are half-wired: forkTrick only says a trick was sidestepped, latentDanger only looks from the student's seat, and pinPressureThreat and threatCheck have no callers.

For the one thinking chain, these computers supply the OBSTACLE (their threat), the GOAL (your tactic), REMOVE-IT (threatAnswer), the TEMPTING CHOICE and THE LINE (tacticalRead, pinPressure) and KNOWLEDGE (the TACTIC_INVARIANT text). But most of their outputs carry squares without the move that executes the tactic, so arrows have to be guessed back out of the sentence.

The biggest missing computer is the first gem's lesson, 'a pin breaks when the pinned piece moves with check'. Nothing computes it, and forkTrick deliberately skips checks.
MISSING: PIN BROKEN WITH CHECK (the first gem's nugget): a pinned piece that can leave the line WITH CHECK or a bigger threat, so the pin is an illusion and the pinning piece hangs. Example: 1.e4 e5 2.Nf3 d6 3.Bc4 Bg4? 4.Bxf7+ Kxf7 5.Ng5+ Ke8 6.Qxg4; also Légal's mate. isRealPin has no such test and forkTrick skips checks. Common below 1500 in open games. Verb: knowledge/consequence.; Sacrifice-with-check followed by a checking fork or pin-break (Bxf7+/Nxf7 decoy families): forkTrick only handles quiet forks after a non-checking capture. Opening-trap frequency. Verb: recognize.; Double threat that is not two pieces: mate threat plus a piece (Qf3 hitting f7-mate and b7), check plus a loose piece. findForks needs two 3+ targets. Common. Verb: recognize.; Live deflection / decoy (attraction) detector outside mating sacs: a forcing move that drags a defender off a square or a duty. Only the theme map and reviewForcedSequence know it. Common in middlegames. Verb: recognize.; Zwischenzug / in-between move: an intermediate check or threat before the expected recapture. Theme-only today. Occasional (1 in ~20 games). Verb: consequence.; Desperado: a doomed piece capturing on its way out instead of being taken for free. Occasional. Verb: consequence.; Interference: a piece dropped on the line between a defender and what it guards. Rare. Verb: recognize.; True x-ray (attack or defence through an enemy piece, e.g. rook defending through the enemy rook on the same file). The name is taken by a discovery detector. Rare. Verb: knowledge.; Clearance (vacating a square or line with tempo) outside mating sacs. Rare. Verb: recognize.; King fork (king attacking two loose pieces in an ending). Excluded by findForks. Endgame, occasional. Verb: recognize.; Trapped piece IN WAITING: a piece one pawn move from being trapped (bishop on a7 vs b6, bishop on g5/h4 vs g5 pushes, Noah's Ark). findTrappedPieces only sees it trapped now. Opening-trap frequency. Verb: prevent.; Latent back-rank weakness: no luft and an enemy heavy piece that could reach the rank in one or two moves (findBackRankWeakness needs a checking invasion now); the 'make luft before it bites' prevention habit. Common. Verb: prevent.; Mate-in-2 threat as a static board fact (findMateThreats and detectNewThreat stop at mate-in-1). Common at club level. Verb: identify.; Pinned piece is not a defender: a defender that is pinned does not really defend, so removal/overload/fork winnability are under-counted. Common. Verb: knowledge.; Counter-attack as an answer to a threat (ignore it with a bigger threat or an in-between check): threatAnswer only knows take/step/pawn-hit/block/guard. Occasional. Verb: prevent.; Fork-square prevention: your two pieces standing on a knight-forkable pair / a square the enemy knight reaches next move (task #30 'Stop-the-fork' still pending). Common below 1400. Verb: prevent.; Poisoned pawn / trapped-queen grab: a pawn capture after which the queen has no safe retreat (b2 grabs). captureHasCounterTactic is partial and unnamed. Opening frequency. Verb: consequence.; Perpetual check as a defensive resource for the worse side (only detected as a PV terminus in onlyMoveSequence). Occasional. Verb: strategize.; Stalemate trick for the LOSING side (stalemateWatch only warns the winner). Rare. Verb: strategize.; Underpromotion with a fork or to avoid stalemate. Very rare. Verb: knowledge.; Windmill (repeated discovered checks harvesting material). Very rare. Verb: knowledge.; Greek-gift soundness checklist (Bxh7+ with Ng5 and Qh5 available, no defending knight on f6). Rare but a classic named pattern. Verb: knowledge.; The OPPONENT-seat latent pin/skewer (their king and queen lined up behind a shield you can remove) — the chance side of latentDanger. Common. Verb: plan.; Latent forks by sliders/queen/pawn (latentFork is knights only), e.g. a pawn fork one push away (e4-e5 hitting two minors). Common in openings. Verb: plan.; Counting attackers vs defenders as a live fact on Learn/Review (exists only in the How-to-Think exchangeChain). Every game. Verb: knowledge/habit.
BROKEN: computeMustDefend (threatOut.ts:92) starts from findHangingPieces (undefended only), so a defended piece attacked by a cheaper piece (a knight hit by a pawn) is never a must-defend; the header (threatOut.ts:4-10) wrongly says SEE-verified. Feeds the importance model's threatNet and the must-defend fact.; weaknessSpine.themesForTactic maps removing_the_guard → ['defensiveMove'] (weaknessSpine.ts:354): a removal-of-the-guard hole is drilled with defensive-move puzzles (coachDrillService:652, CoachTeachPage:2784). Forward map has capturingDefender→removing_the_guard and attraction→deflection (tacticClassifierService.ts:55-56), so the round trip breaks.; Six pin implementations: findPins, detectPin, findPinFrom (isRealPin); nextPlans.isPinnedPiece:118 (absolute only); reviewMoveTeaching.pinnedSquares:156 (no canLeaveLine, so a file-pinned pawn counts and 'Unpins your pawn' can be spoken); factStakes.isScenicPawnPin:137 as a second 'does it bite' rule.; Fork defined at least six ways: findForks (winnable + forker-safety, minors count), tacticClassifier.detectFork:155 (no gates at all, used by scanUpcomingTactics for Play alerts and the PV scan), verifyForkOnBoard, detectNewThreat (K/Q/R victims only), setupTrainerService.moveIsTheFork:139, forkTrick.forkAfter, pvPlayback.tacticWinsMaterial.; Two skewer definitions disagree: findSkewers (tacticsDetector.ts:255) never checks the back piece is worth taking (detectSkewer :257 does); pvPlayback.tacticWinsMaterial's skewer branch (pvPlayback.ts:178) tests the FRONT piece or a king behind, which a findSkewers skewer can never have, so the gate passes nearly everything.; Three discovered-attack definitions: findDiscoveredAttacks (R/Q/K target + tempo), detectDiscovery (minor targets, no tempo), and positionReadingService.findXrays:818, which is a discovery-in-waiting under the name 'x-ray' — clashing with TacticType 'x_ray' (attack through an enemy piece), so the fact never joins the discovered_attack weakness.; Dead: tacticalRead.computeTacticalRead (:265) no callers; threatCheck.buildThreatCheckQuestion/judgeThreatCheckPick (:42/:102) no callers; pinPressure.isPinPressureMove (:129) and pinPressureThreat (:138) no callers; tacticalProfileService.computeTacticalProfile no callers (isTacticWeakness always false); tacticClassifierService.getTacticMotifStats (:483) no callers; missedTacticService private detectPin/detectSkewer/detectDiscoveredAttack/detectRemovingTheGuard/detectOverloadedPiece/detectTrappedPiece/detectClearance/detectDoubleCheck/detectBackRank/detectXRay (:204-:648) reachable only from test-only legacyTacticGeometry (:818).; forkTrick is half-wired: only trickSidestepped has callers; forkTrickFor's opportunity (the student CAN play the trick) is never taught. It also skips any capture that gives check (forkTrick.ts:97), so the Bxf7+ Kxf7 Ng5+ family is outside it.; latentDanger is student-seat only (latentDanger.ts:87): the same alignment against the opponent's king/queen (the student's chance) is not computed — half of a dual-use computer.; detectMissedTactics: capped at 10 (missedTacticService.ts:14, :1013, a G4.5 cap); classifies with no PV (:997) so multi-move tactics become 'combination'; generateExplanation (:930) prints 'pawn advantage' numbers; CoachGameReview.tsx:4351-4354 shows the raw enum via replace(/_/g,' ') and '(x points)' instead of tacticWord.; liveTacticsContext cuts immediate/threats/opportunities to 5 (liveTacticsContext.ts:107, :108, :370) — a cap on computed facts that also feed spoken alerts; conceptForLine default .slice(0, 3) (conceptEngine.ts:752).; Rating decides volume: getTacticLookahead (tacticAlertService.ts:296-299) and isCriticalThreat's bar (:405-407) scale by rating, against the foundation rule that rating sets strength, not how much the coach says.; findForks skips the king as a forker (tacticsDetector.ts:126), so king forks in endgames are never seen.; Defender counts in findForks, findRemovableGuards, findOverloadedPieces use chess.attackers, which counts a PINNED defender as a defender (false negatives).; findMateThreats description says 'White/Black has mate in one' (tacticsDetector.ts:356) — colour names, a perspective-rule risk wherever it is spoken unre-voiced.; Output gaps for arrows (S10): HangingPiece and findHangingBySee carry no capturer square; computeMustDefend carries the attacker's TYPE not square; TacticPattern carries no executing move (discovery's tempo move, removal's taker, overload's attackers, trapped piece's attackers); looseTrigger returns prose only.; tacticClaimValidator.stripUngroundedTacticSentences is a validator on model output — by the G0 test its existence means the model still chooses tactic words on that path (GameChatPanel).; computerRoles tactic lane records only a generic 'missed-tactic' misconception tag (computerRoles.ts:48) while the analysis spine is per motif — two granularities for the same miss.

## Plans and structure: deriveNextPlans, structurePlan, planRace, planArc, namedPawnStructure, mastersPlanRead, pawn-structure reads
This area has many computers but no ONE plan computer. The 'what's the plan' question is answered by about seven producers on different surfaces: structurePlan (Learn/Play through positionFacts, Review thesis, chat), deriveNextPlans (Review, chat, Learn's learnMoveTeaching), namedPawnStructure (Learn structure lane, chat), mastersPlanRead (Learn), the reviewStrategicOrientation beats (Review), planChoice and planArc (Learn only), and positionalRead (Learn quiet plies). They overlap. The passer and isolani plans are each said by 3-4 producers in different words, and dedupe relies on ideaKey strings. The wing, passer and near-promotion definitions disagree between files.

For the one thinking chain (F0c/F18), these computers naturally fill GOAL (structurePlan / deriveNextPlans / mastersPlanRead), OBSTACLE (weak pawns, worst piece, colour complex), REMOVE-IT (breaks, blockade, reroute), TEMPTING CHOICE (planChoice, planRace's 'you can't just race') and LINE (lookaheadPlan / planArc). None of them is composed into that chain today: each speaks its own sentence.

Two structural defects block S10 and the loop:
- (1) The plan producers return prose without the squares and moves they name. deriveNextPlans, structurePlan, planRaceClause, namedPawnStructure and planChoice all drop the squares, so plan talk is not arrowed.
- (2) Plan computers are teach-only. computerRoles marks structure, openingIdea and planArc as na, so the heat map never learns whether a student follows a structure's plan.

The fix direction:
- One plan fact type that carries kind, side, squares and line.
- One structure inventory (describeStructure) with one wing and passer rule.
- Plan computers emit to the decider instead of each surface.
- Add the missing nuggets: the rule of the square in races, the chain base, two weaknesses, prophylaxis.
MISSING: The rule of the square in a general race: can the defending king catch a runner (with pieces on, or several pawns)? It would feed planRace instead of the 'nobody interferes' hedge.; A candidate-passer / majority-conversion computer: which pawn of a healthy majority becomes the passer, and the push order (Capablanca's 'the unopposed pawn first').; A generic pawn-chain base computer: find any locked chain, its base and head, and the lever that hits the base (…c5 vs d4, f4-f5 vs e6). Today only two fixed square patterns are recognised.; Plans for named structures beyond the four: Carlsbad (minority attack b4-b5), Maroczy (restrain …d5/…b5), Hedgehog (…b5/…d5 breaks), Stonewall (the e5/e4 hole and the bad bishop), Benoni (e4-e5 vs …b5), Sicilian Scheveningen/Boleslavsky (the d5 hole), Slav/QGD exchange.; Strategic prophylaxis: their best lever or plan square, and the move that stops it before it happens (F01 PREVENT). opponentIntentRead is tactical only.; The principle of two weaknesses: when one target is held, create a second on the other wing.; Creating an outpost: which enemy pawn move to provoke, or which defending pawn to trade, to make a hole.; Pawns vs your bishop colour: fix their pawns on your bishop's colour; put your pawns opposite your bishop. findPieceQuality knows a bad bishop but no plan fixes it.; Break readiness: count the supporters vs the defenders of a break square before pushing (the arithmetic nugget applied to levers). moveTiming / 'mistimed-pawn-break' judges after the fact; nothing prepares the break.; Which file a break opens, and whose rook gets it first (it would join findPawnBreaks with planRace's file collision).; The pawn-storm race with opposite-side castling: count pawn moves to contact on each king (both sides' storm tempi). Today only an arrow beat exists.; Space vs overextension: when a space edge becomes a target (pawns too far forward, the squares behind them).; A plan-level record: the student played on the wrong wing, or against the structure's plan, so 'no-plan' / 'pawn-structure' gets BROKEN rows from the structure computers, not only from moveIntent.; Pawn islands as a teachable count. describeStructure computes islands and only lookaheadPlan:1347 reads them; nothing teaches 'fewer islands is better'.; The breakthrough sac in pawn endings, live: findBreakthrough exists but is only used by tests.

## Trades, piece quality, material counting, exchange ledger
This area holds the coach's arithmetic: who won what, whether a trade was a good deal, which piece is good or bad, and whether a sacrifice paid. The pieces of a strong core already exist. `exchangeLedger.proofCut` (src/services/exchangeLedger.ts:314) cuts a line down to the moves that prove one material claim. The pin-aware SEE (`legalSeeGainFor`, positionReadingService.ts:212) and the settled count (`material.settledBalance`, material.ts:42) feed it. Together they can fill THE LINE and TEMPTING-CHOICE-FAILS roles of the thinking chain (F18), and the gem bar (F04, "at least a piece") depends on them.

Around that core there are many disagreeing copies:
- 7 producers of "bad or worst piece".
- 4 definitions of "bad bishop".
- 2 trade verdicts with different thresholds (Learn uses tradeJudgement, Review uses readTrade).
- 5 places that say "trade when ahead", with different bars.
- 4 bishop-pair tests.
- 3 "count before you take" computers: one pin-blind, two legal.
- 2 sacrifice tests that disagree on what counts as net.
- 4 ways of settling material.
- Raw, unsettled counts still feed chat, How-to-Think, the LLM prompts and a claim-stripper gate.
- About 50 private piece-value tables remain beside `pieceValues.ts`.

Most outputs are prose strings with no moves attached: recapture, trade, sacrifice, trade-consequence, materialPlan, and the proof text. So arrows (S10) would have to be re-derived from the sentence. The Learn trade and recapture lanes push `arrows: []` while naming a move.

For the one brain, the job is to merge each group onto the settled ledger and the legal SEE, and to have every result carry its squares and SAN moves. Each verdict should then become a role-tagged fact (REASON / TEMPTING CHOICE / LINE / HABIT) for the single composer. Missing knowledge to compute includes imbalance teaching, which pieces to trade, and exchange-sacrifice recognition.
MISSING: IMBALANCE TEACHING (quality vs quantity): rook vs two minors, queen vs two rooks, queen vs three minors, two minors vs rook+pawn, and which side the position favours (open vs closed, pawns on both wings). Today endgameMatchup classifies only endgame matchups. Example: after an exchange that gives R+P vs B+N in a middlegame. Frequency: roughly 1 game in 20. Verbs: knowledge, strategize.; WHICH PIECE TO TRADE WHEN DEFENDING / ATTACKING: trade their ATTACKERS when under attack, keep your own attackers and avoid trades when attacking, a queen trade kills an attack. Example: their Ng5 and Qh5 on your king; offer the Be7 for the knight. Common (every attack). Verbs: prevent, strategize.; DON'T TRADE YOUR FIANCHETTO BISHOP / the king's-guard minor: trading the g7 bishop leaves dark-square holes round a castled king (the Yugoslav Be3-Qd2-Bh6 idea). Example: Sicilian Dragon Bh6 vs Bxh6. Frequent in openings. Verbs: recognize, prevent.; KNIGHT VS BISHOP BY STRUCTURE: closed centre and pawns on one colour favour the knight; open board and pawns on both wings favour the bishop. This is the judgement behind a N-for-B trade. Example: French Advance ...Bb4xc3. Frequent in openings. Verbs: knowledge, strategize.; GOOD BISHOP / BAD BISHOP DIRECTIONAL LESSON: put your pawns on the opposite colour of your remaining bishop. Today the coach only flags an already-bad bishop. Example: after trading the light bishop, pawns go to light squares. Common. Verbs: plan, knowledge.; EXCHANGE SACRIFICE (positional): Rxc3 to wreck the structure or win dark squares, judged as a type rather than a generic 'sacrifice'. Only general compensation exists (reviewSacrifice.ts:140). Example: Sicilian ...Rxc3. Frequency: 1 in 50 games. Verbs: recognize, strategize.; OPPOSITE-COLOURED BISHOPS IN THE MIDDLEGAME favour the attacker (each side can't defend the other colour). conceptEngine.ts:155 knows only the endgame 'drawish' fact. Example: Bd3 vs ...Be7 with the queens on and the kingside open. Frequency: 1 in 30. Verbs: knowledge, strategize.; DESPERADO / IN-BETWEEN CAPTURE IN AN EXCHANGE: a doomed piece takes something on its way out, or a zwischenzug (check or threat) before recapturing changes the exchange arithmetic. lineGiftIndex only exempts the desperado; nothing TEACHES it. Example: Nxe5 Nxe5 d4 with an intermezzo. Frequency: 1 in 15 games. Verbs: recognize, consequence.; CAPTURE ORDER / WHICH PIECE STARTS THE EXCHANGE: take with the least valuable piece first, and on a doubly attacked square start with the piece that is not needed elsewhere. thinkingExchangeChain asks 'who takes first' only in How-to-Think; Learn and Review never teach it on a real capture. Common. Verbs: knowledge, habit.; ADDING AN ATTACKER BEFORE TAKING (pile-up / 'not yet, first this'): a capture loses now but wins after one quiet move adds an attacker or removes a defender. This is the 'remove it' chain role applied to exchanges. Example: Rd1 doubles on d4 before Nxd4. Frequent. Verbs: plan, consequence.; SIMPLIFICATION HAZARD WHEN AHEAD: a trade that converts into a drawn ending (wrong-colour bishop + rook pawn, rook endings with all pawns on one side, opposite bishops). Today 'trade when ahead' is unconditional. Example: up a pawn, trading into R+3 vs R+2 on one wing. Frequency: 1 in 40. Verbs: consequence, knowledge.; WHAT MATERIAL CAN FORCE MATE (F01 example): K+N+N cannot force mate, K+B+N can, a bishop + wrong rook pawn is a draw, two connected passers beat a rook in some cases. Only partially in endgame lessons; no live computer. Example: trading into K+NN vs K. Rare (1 in 200) but named in F01. Verbs: knowledge.; TRADE TO RELIEVE A CRAMPED POSITION: the side with less space wants trades, the side with more space avoids them. No space-to-trade link exists. Example: KID or Hedgehog Black looking for exchanges. Common. Verbs: strategize.; PIECE HAS NO PURPOSE / REDUNDANT PIECE: two pieces doing the same job (two knights eyeing one square), so trade one off or reroute. Example: Nf3 and Nd2 both wanting e5. Frequent. Verbs: plan.; PAWN TRADE VS PIECE TRADE WHEN AHEAD: trade pieces, not pawns (pawns are the winning material). conversionMethod states the step but no live computer flags a pawn trade that halves the winning chances. Example: up a pawn, playing a pawn exchange on the queenside. Common in endgames. Verbs: strategize, consequence.; RECAPTURE TOWARD THE CENTRE AS A HABIT across surfaces: recaptureChoice exists only in Learn. Review and Play-on-ask never compare recaptures. This is a parity gap rather than a new computer. Verbs: habit.; LOOSE-PIECE AFTER A TRADE (the recapturing piece is left undefended, setting up a later fork or skewer): there is no forward check of what a recapture leaves loose. Example: ...Qxd5 recapture, then Nc3 with tempo. Frequent at under 1400. Verbs: prevent, consequence.; MATERIAL ARITHMETIC NUGGET: 'two pawns plus initiative for a piece' and 'three pawns ≈ a piece' when judging gambits and sacrifices. No computer states a pawn-count exchange rate. Example: a Greek-gift-type sac regaining two pawns. Frequency: 1 in 30. Verbs: knowledge.
BROKEN: DEAD: exchangeLedger.exchangeNetForLine (src/services/exchangeLedger.ts:209) has no production caller, only exchangeLedger.test.ts:5.; STALE COMMENT: pieceValueRead.ts:16 still describes playCommentary.opponentsBestPiece, which playCommentary.ts:83 says was REMOVED 2026-08-26.; CAP (G4.5): findPieceQuality ends with `.slice(0, 4)` (positionReadingService.ts:616), so a 5th good or bad piece is dropped by count, not by worth. tradeQuality and findTradeTarget read this truncated list, so a bad piece past the 4th can never start a trade lesson.; PIN-BLIND SEE still live: factStakes.exchangeStakes uses positionReadingService.seeGain (factStakes.ts:85), although positionReadingService.ts:154 names legalSeeGain as its replacement. This is the exact false sentence material.ts:5-20 was written to end. Both also speak point totals rather than pieces.; UNSETTLED MATERIAL to the LLM: liveTacticsContext.describeMaterialBalance (:283) and coachPrompts.computeMaterialBalance (:993) are raw.; G0 GATE: materialClaimValidator.stripDisprovenMaterialSentences (wired at coachAnswerGates.ts:226) is a post-hoc claim-stripper of the kind G0 bans, and it grades against the RAW count. A true 'level after the recapture' sentence can be stripped mid-exchange.; moveInsight.materialPlan uses the private raw moveInsight.material (:929) for its ±3 and up-the-exchange branches, so it can say 'You are three down — keep pieces on' one ply before the recapture.; S10 ARROW GAP: the Learn recapture lane and trade lane push `arrows: []` (learnBoardTeaching.ts:157, :230) although recaptureChoice names two SAN moves and tradeJudgement names traded squares. recaptureChoice, sacrificeCompensation, describeSimplifyingTrade, describeTradeConsequence, materialPlan, pawnEndingTrade, proofAgainstMover and proofForMover return prose only (moves embedded in text), so arrows can only be re-derived from the sentence.; CONFLICTING VERDICTS on one trade: tradeJudgement calls a bishop bad with 3+ centre pawns c-f on its colour and no forward-ray test (tradeJudgement.ts:80-84). findPieceQuality, badEnemyBishop and principleAttribution require 4+ pawns and blocked forward rays. Learn can praise 'giving away a bad bishop' that Review calls an active bishop.; TRADE THRESHOLDS DIVERGE: 'ahead' is settled material ≥2 (tradeJudgement, readTrade, assembleTradeAnswer), eval ≥150cp (describeSimplifyingTrade, describeTradeConsequence), raw ≥3 / CONVERSION_EDGE 3 (materialPlan, readConversion). 'Defender' is within 2 squares of the king (tradeJudgement:91) vs covers 2+ king squares under fire (tradeQuality:51). 'Poor' trade needs cpLoss ≥50 (tradeJudgement) vs ≥30 (readTrade).; SACRIFICE NET DIVERGES: factStakes.isSacrifice subtracts the captured piece (:170); groundedAnswer.sacrificeOffer does not (:1844). One move can be a sac in chat and not in Review.; BISHOP PAIR DIVERGES: positionReadingService.bishopPair and playCommentary.gainedBishopPair ignore minor-piece equality; reviewConcepts.detectTwoBishops requires it (:259). None of them checks that the two bishops are on opposite colours (it is assumed).; MID-EXCHANGE has 5 rules: coachFeatureService.pendingRecapture (any capture back), boardState.inFluxAfter (SEE ≥0), material.settledBalance, exchangeLedger.settled, conversionMethod's hang subtraction. Two exported functions share the name `pendingRecapture` with different signatures and rules (coachFeatureService.ts:4814, utils/justCaptured.ts:24).; VALUE TABLES: about 50 private `{p:1,n:3,b:3,r:5,q:9,...}` tables remain (e.g. exchangeLedger.ts:43 and :422, positionReadingService.ts:31, groundedAnswer.ts:67/:3156/:7036, coachFeatureService.ts:1748, CoachTeachPage.tsx:8701), with king values of 0, 99 or 100 or missing. pieceValues.ts was created to end this (its header counts 53).

## Openings: book departure, opening identity, theory, masters references, pro repertoires, pro-game-references lookup
For the one brain, the opening is where the record starts and where the student's level shows up first. The parts are already built: the opening's name, its identity (what it provokes, what it aims for, gambit, sharp or quiet), the masters' plan and break, the popular alternative and the line that refutes it, the departure verdict, and real games. They are not one computer. The worst problem is "did this move leave book?". At least seven judgments answer it, using four sources and thresholds of 20, 20, 10, 8, the name DB's prefix rule, and a ply-6 floor: bookDeparture, theoryDeparture, theoryDeviationScan, the lecture's departurePly, isBookLine, principleAttribution #35, and buildOpeningMoveDetail. So Learn and Review can name different plies, and a move counted as book (isBookLine) can also be called "you left the book" (bookDeparture). Opening names are read two ways: Learn uses the FEN index (openingPositions), Review uses a one-signature transposition table, so the same game can get two names. Pro and master games are a mix of LLM tools, Review's one modelGameClause from the exact position, identity's two famous games, and a Learn walkthrough that breaks F0d ("his pick in 4 of 6 games"). Nothing chooses a game through the decider. Pro references have no position index, so the coach can't cite a game "like this one" unless it is the same opening. Most of these computers return strings. bookDeparture's departing move and usual move never reach an arrow. One live defect: Review's buildOpeningMoveDetail reads the reference coach's own play DB and tells the student "your bread-and-butter, it's scored well for you".
MISSING: ONE book oracle: a single 'in book / left book, who, where, the usual move, how common at the student's band' fact, used by every surface and by the 'book' classification (replaces the 7 judgments). Serves knowledge; role = knowledge/tempting-choice.; Position-fingerprint index into the 2,209 pro-game references and the masters top games (pawn skeleton + castling sides + material + phase), so the coach can say 'Carlsen reached this same structure against X' outside exact opening positions. Today refs have no FEN field (keys: eco/openingId/variation/pgn…). Rare per move, frequent per game. Serves recognize/knowledge.; Decider-chosen master/pro game reference for Learn and lessons: pick a real game where the same idea worked or the same slip was punished, said once. Today only Review's modelGameClause (exact position) and identity's famous list exist. Serves knowledge.; How their sideline is punished: when the OPPONENT leaves book with a dubious move, what masters (or the engine) play against it and why. theirOpeningVerdict names only the cost and the move they should have played. Example: 1.e4 e5 2.Nf3 d6 3.Bc4 Bg4? -> what punishes it. Every few games at 800-1400. Serves consequence/plan.; Move-order transposition trap: 'this order lets them into line X you don't play / avoids Y'. Detects that the student's order permits a transposition into a different named opening, or out of their home line, that a sibling order denies. Example: 1.d4 Nf6 2.Nf3 versus 2.c4 (allows or avoids the Nimzo). About 1 in 20 games. Serves prevent/strategize.; Opening principle tempo ledger with compensation: 'you're a pawn down but 3 tempi ahead in development' live in gambits. Identity's gambit fact is offline main-line only; tempoTeaching counts one piece's moves. Example: Danish/Smith-Morra after 5 moves. Serves consequence/knowledge.; Break prerequisite: 'not yet — the c5 break needs ...Nc6 first because d4 is defended twice'. mastersPlanRead names the break and share but not what must be prepared (count attackers vs defenders on the break square). Common. Serves plan / remove-it.; Opponent-tendency at the student's level: 'at your level, 40% of opponents answer your Pirc with 4.f4; here's what to know'. homeOpeningSteer has the trie but only uses it to choose the bot's moves, never to teach. Serves prevent/strategize.; Repertoire inconsistency detector: the student plays different moves from the same position across their games (no settled line), or plays a system that clashes with their other choices. Every student with 20+ games. Serves strategize/identify.; Recurring departure in Learn live: departureRecordSentence exists only in the Review intro; Learn does not say 'you left book at this same board last game'. Serves identify/record.; Opening-specific piece trap/bad-piece origin: 'this opening tends to leave your light-squared bishop bad (French)', computed from the main-line pawn skeleton against bishop colour. Common in French/Caro/Stonewall. Serves recognize/strategize.; Early queen sortie tempo-loss as a theory fact: 'the queen out early gets hit with tempo — Nc3 gains a move' as a book-level consequence (Scandinavian, early Qh5). identity 'provokes' partly covers the reply but not the tempo count. Serves consequence.; Opening-to-endgame link: the typical ending the opening's structure leads to (computed from master games' final structures, as G9.1 does offline), so the coach can say 'this IQP tends to become a bad-bishop ending'. Rare per game. Serves strategize.; Theory currency/soundness tag for the line being played (engine eval at the master main-line terminus from the student's side: 'this line is objectively dubious but tricky'). The soundness sweep is offline only for lessons. Serves knowledge/consequence.; Name-DB vs masters disagreement detector (an opening named as book that masters never play, or masters' main move with no name). Would catch the isBookLine class automatically. Infrastructure, rare.
BROKEN: FALSE CLAIM ABOUT THE STUDENT (live, Review): reviewStrategicOrientation.buildOpeningMoveDetail (:48-76) reads lookupHisPlaySync, the REFERENCE COACH's corpus (danya-play-db), and tells the student 'This is your bread-and-butter — you play it almost every time here … it's scored well for you at N%' / 'here you almost always play X'. Reached as the per-move fallback at coachFeatureService.ts:3191 whenever moveBriefing is null.; F0d VIOLATIONS: proForkWalkthrough.ts:33 'his pick in N of M games', :34 'his choice here', :135 'Here's how {proName} plays the {opening}, from N of his real games', :137 'his games split', :145 "That's {proName}'s {opening} — his real choices". The inline prose at CoachTeachPage.tsx:4331-4335 adds 'I'll tell you why he chose each' and 'Tap Play or step through with the arrows' (talks about the interface). It also assumes every pro is male.; BOOK CONTRADICTION ON ONE LEARN MOVE: CoachTeachPage:9180 suppresses the post-move grade with isBookLine (name DB), while the announcement at :8413 uses bookDeparture (masters). One move can be treated as book by one and as 'You left the book' by the other. bookDeparture.ts:4-9 records that isBookLine is the wrong test, yet it still drives gameAnalysisService's 'book' classification (:1369/:1637/:2045), gameImportUtils:126, the mistakePuzzleService:475 exemption and the positionFacts:865 praise gate.; Up to five departure plies in ONE review: findTheoryDeparture card (20/0-games rule), lecture departurePly (<10, reviewOpeningTheory.ts:186), theoryDeviationScan (>=8, plus unbanded amateur), theirOpeningVerdict/bookDeparture (per-move <20), principleAttribution #35 (name DB, ply>=6).; bookDeparture's rationale says 'Not the 37 MB local file — parsing that on a phone is the memory spike iOS kills apps for' (bookDeparture.ts:10-13), but Learn loads exactly that file through learnBoardTeaching.ts:618 (openingPlanTeaching -> ensureMastersDbLoaded) and the generator does at openingGenerator.ts:2146. masterMovesCachedSync then falls through to it (masterPlayLookup.ts:215).; DEAD: openingNameClaimValidator.ts (0 production importers; only its own test and registry.completeness.test.ts:123). A validator, so banned under G0 anyway.; DEAD: openingDetectionService.isStillInOpening (:268), 0 production callers.; Transposition reader split: detectOpeningTranspositional holds ONE hand-written signature (openingDetectionService.ts:216, Grand Prix). The FEN index (openingPositions) is used only on Learn, while Review/chat/reviewOpeningTheory use the one-signature version, so the same game can be named differently on two surfaces.; masterPlayWatcher (grounding Layer A) is mounted only in OpeningPlayMode.tsx:398. CoachTeachPage runs its own two unmemoized explorer fetches per FEN change (1512-1513, bypassing masterPlayCache) to build an LLM snapshot (lichessSnapshotRef), and nothing warms the amateur cache in Review, so refutedAlternative's amateur candidates (refutedAlternative.ts:141) are cold there.; pro-game references held twice: Dexie proGameReferences (seeded dataLoader.ts:762-775, read only by ProGamesPage via proGameReferenceService) and the in-memory fetch (proGameReferenceData). Other proGameReferenceService exports (getGameReferencesForProOpening/ForOpening/count/store) have 0 production callers.; Identity famous games capped with .slice(0, 2) (openingIdentity.ts:144). The census also notes masterPlay explorer tools slice(0,5) and the CoachTeachPage snapshot slice(0,3) on topGames. Review lecture citation is once per lecture (by design).; No arrows from opening facts: openingAnnouncement / theirOpeningVerdict return strings only (openingAnnouncement.ts:54,166), dropping BookDeparture.san/mainSan/fen. openingIdentityTeaching returns arrows: [] (learnBoardTeaching.ts:641) although provokes.reply is a move. tempoTeaching also arrows: [] (:355). buildOpeningMoveDetail and strongChoice carry no squares (strongChoice returns san but the lane sets squares: [], learnBoardTeaching.ts:189).; Numbers where a reason should be: openingRecordClause '49% so far' (openingRecordBeat.ts:33); lecture intro 'rests on N master games' (reviewOpeningTheory.ts:651); buildOpeningMoveDetail '…at N%', 'reach this in N games'.; theoryDeparture claims the student's-level move with a rating band (explorerBandFor), but theoryDeviationScan's amateur fallback (amateurPlayLookup) is unbanded 1600-2500. A 900-rated student is measured against 1600+ play there.; Chat still free-composes over master/DB grounding: buildOpeningDbEntries is injected into the prompt (coachApi.ts:1844, :6552) and the master/player-game tools are LLM-chosen. Game citation is decided by the model, not the one decider.
COMPUTERS: bookDeparture (src/services/bookDeparture.ts:49) — First ply that left theory: played move <20 master games at that position (masterMovesCachedSync); if the masters miss, name-DB isBookLine HIT=in book; returns {ply,san,mover,mainSan,fen}. Scans 60 plies. [knowledge/knowledge] DUP of theoryDeparture.findTheoryDeparture, theoryDeviationScan, reviewOpeningTheory departurePly, isBookLine, principleAttribution left-book-early | theoryDeparture.findTheoryDeparture + walkBookLine (src/services/theoryDeparture.ts:73, :168) — Departure = the first position with 0 master games, provided the previous position had >=20 (a DIFFERENT definition from bookDeparture: a move with 1-19 master games counts as book here and as left-book there). Returns mainMove with pct, the top 3 moves, and the student's-band top move (amateur explorer). Scans 30 plies. walkBookLine = greedy masters main line. ## TRAPS AND GEMS — the ~8 trap systems and why no gem has ever reached David's ears
Gem DATA is fine: 389 rows (344 masterclass + 45 gambit), 388 weapon-tier, and all 388 carry hand narration, so `isSurfaceableGem` (punishGems.ts:66) passes every one. The UNLOCK no longer blocks anything: `areWeaponsUnlocked` returns true (wlppLadder.ts:62). So the opening page Watch/Learn/Play on a gem works when it is tapped. The gem never REACHES the student on its own for three reasons, all in code.
(1) LIVE LEARN: a weapon gem fires only if the COACH plays the slip (`findLivePunishment`, CoachTeachPage:8487). The coach plays a slip only through `pickTaughtSlip`. That needs `slipsAllowed`, which on the default 'medium' (appStore.ts:242) means a student under 1000 (coachGameEngine.ts:239-249). David is about 1300, so it never fires for him. On top of that, when an opening is named, book moves win first (CoachTeachPage:7645-7652). The slip also fires only if the current board is EXACTLY a gem spine (median 12 plies), and nothing steers the game there.
(2) The walkthrough gem picker says nothing until a chip is tapped (useTeachWalkthrough:1199-1240). Baking gems into the tree also kills the Watch aside (:1252).
(3) Review needs a real opponent to play the exact slip.
So the only thing he may have heard is the WARNING side (`trapAheadTeaching`).
Beyond that: eight systems use three different bars for what counts as a trap (gems ≥+0.5, finder ≥2 pawns of material, trapDetector ≤−200cp with ≥40 games), and none of them is F04's "a piece or mate". The weapon side records nothing to the student record, which is half-built.
MISSING: A gem steerer: a computer that, while the student is still in book, picks a coach reply (inside the strength window, from the band's own moves) that heads TOWARD the nearest gem spine, so a weapon gem can actually arise. Today teachableSlipAt only reacts to a position it lands on.; Pin-holds-only-while-the-pinned-piece-cannot-move-with-check (the false or relative pin): detects that a 'pinned' piece can leave WITH CHECK or MATE, or with a bigger capture, so the pin breaks and the pinner hangs. Examples: the brief's 1.e4 e5 2.Nf3 d6 3.Bc4 Bg4? 4.Bxf7+ Kxf7 5.Ng5+ Ke8 6.Qxg4, and Legal's mate. Rare, but THE opening nugget. Verb: knowledge/recognize. (Only 'unpin' and 'breaks the pin' exist: deliberation.ts:453, reviewMoveTeaching.ts:205.); F04 trap-bar classifier: ONE computer that decides 'is this a trap' as 'a natural move at the student's band that loses to a FORCED, engine-proven line winning at least a piece or mate within N moves'. It would replace the four bars above. It must also check FORCEDNESS (every opponent reply in the line loses), which nothing checks today.; Bxf7+/Bxf2+ sacrifice detector: f7/f2 is defended only by the king, a minor piece is aimed at it, and the sacrifice wins a piece or mate via a knight check or a fork. The most common amateur trap motif. Verb: recognize.; Early queen-trap detector: a queen that has wandered (…Qxb2, Qxb7, an early Qh5) with only 0-1 safe squares after the opponent's natural reply. Examples: the Poisoned b-pawn, the Siberian, Qb6 walking into Nb5/Nc7. Verb: recognize/prevent.; Opening-name trap recognizer: names the classic trap the board matches (Légal, Fishing Pole, Noah's Ark, Elephant, Lasker, Siberian, Blackburne Shilling, Englund, Kieninger, Halosar) BY POSITION and motif, not by curated lesson. Today names exist only in 5 lesson files and in trapLine strings.; A trap-in-review-of-avoidance computer: 'you avoided X, which loses to Y', for the student's own book moves when the student played the correct move at a gem position. This is a GREEN record for the heat map. reviewFullData does voice it, but it records nothing.; A punish-found / punish-missed capability row: the weapon twin of trapLearning, so a gem the student found goes green and a missed one goes red per trap position.; Gambit-acceptance consequence: the student grabbed a gambit pawn and must now count development tempi versus material. A golden-arithmetic nugget for gambit lines (King's Gambit, Danish, Smith-Morra). Verb: consequence/strategize.; A tempo-gaining queen-chase detector: an early opponent queen in the centre that can be hit with developing moves (Scandinavian Qxd5 Nc3, Center Game). Teaches the 'develop with tempo' habit. Verb: plan.; Back-rank / smothered-mate opening traps: matePatterns has named mates (matePatterns.ts:192 has legals-mate), but no computer asks 'is a NAMED mate one natural opponent move away' to feed the trapAhead lane.; A 'which side of the board is the trap on' seat check for trap lines: the punisher's side must equal the student's seat (CLAUDE.md seat rule). Gems compute it at runtime (gemCrushLines gemsForPosition), but repertoire and pro trapLines rely on curation only.
BROKEN: DECISIVE: the weapon gem cannot reach David in a live game. pickTaughtSlip is the only way the opponent plays a gem slip, and slipsAllowed returns false on the default 'medium' for any student at 1000 or above (coachGameEngine.ts:239-249; appStore.ts:242). He is about 1300. The code comment at coachGameEngine.ts:672-683 records gem_alert_spoken at 0 events in 63 sessions.; Learn's named-opening book layer (CoachTeachPage:7645-7652) returns the DB book move before getAdaptiveMove / pickTeachingReply ever runs, so on a taught line the coach can never walk into a gem.; teachableSlipAt only reacts to an EXACT gem spine position (median 12 plies, max 26). Nothing steers the game toward a gem position, so reaching one is chance.; The walkthrough gem picker pauses the walk and speaks nothing (useTeachWalkthrough:1199-1240). A gem is heard only after the student taps a 'Trap N' chip.; computeWatchGemAside is effectively dead in the walkthrough: bakeGemsIntoTree (CoachTeachPage:1049/5681) bakes a picker on every node where the aside would fire, and the aside is skipped when hasBakedGems (useTeachWalkthrough:1252-1255). It survives only at a transposed duplicate node, because the bake dedupes a gem across the tree.; The weapons lock UI is dead: areWeaponsUnlocked always returns true (wlppLadder.ts:62), so weapons-locked-card, the 'expert pass' button and WEAPONS_LOCK_HINT (OpeningDetailPage:2228-2251; wlppLadder.ts:101) can never render. The unlock blocker the brief cites was retired on 2026-09-02. The expert-pass button also needs two taps to commit (OpeningDetailPage:1602), which likely explains the June probe that 'pressed unlock and no gem surfaced'.; 20 gambit gems are orphaned from every opening page: gambit-kings-gambit/evans/budapest records are deleted as RETIRED_GAMBIT_DUPES (dataLoader.ts:636-646) while the gems still carry those ids.; proRepertoireService.findTrapTilesForCanonicalLine has no production caller (proRepertoireService.ts:118). It also returns [] whenever LEARN_SIMPLIFIED is set (:121).; trap-engine-verification.json gates nothing at runtime. Only a test reads it, and it marks all 57 lines 'keep', including 'trap' lines the engine scores at −45 to +30cp.; repertoire-trap-classifications.json has no src reader (only scripts/audit-repertoire-orientation.mjs).; OpeningPlayMode scopes findLivePunishment to opening.id (OpeningPlayMode:435; gemCrushLines `!openingId || g.openingId === openingId`), so a gem from another opening that transposes into the game is ignored.; The weapon side of a gem writes no capabilityEvidence row: gemResolution (CoachTeachPage:9093-9117) gives a reward and a captureEvent only. A gem the student found or missed never reaches the heat map, which breaks the dual-use rule. The warning side records only under the generic 'missed-opponents-threat' tag (trapLearning.ts:23).; Four different definitions of a trap: gem tier ≥+0.5 eval (punishGems.ts:57); gemFinder ≥2 pawns of material (gemFinder.ts:54); trapDetector ≤−200cp with ≥40 games (openingTrapDetector.ts:20-25); pro trapLines classified by hand (trap-line-classifications.json). None of them is F04's 'a piece or mate'.; There is no Bishop's Opening gem and no Philidor 3…Bg4 Bxf7+ gem. The only e4 e5 Nf3 d6 gems are two 'Bg5? Nxe4' gems at 93-109cp.; Shipped trap names leak player names, e.g. 'Firouzja victim', 'Hans Niemann 3161', 'Wesley So victim' (trap-line-classifications keys / pro-repertoires trapLines names). F0d requires crediting the game only.
