## Endgame technique, tablebase, geometry and arithmetic nuggets (rule of the square, opposition, counting, races, mates)
This area is the strongest truth layer the coach owns. It is weak at reaching the student and at handing over squares.

Every named technique except the dead triangulation and breakthrough detectors goes through one router, conceptEngine.endgameConceptFor. Ten geometry theorems in endgameTechnique.ts sit behind it, with the endgameMatchup classes as the general fallback. That is the right shape for the one chain: each technique is a KNOWLEDGE or GOAL role. The tablebase can supply THE LINE and the TEMPTING CHOICE (gradeEndgameMove's threw-win and threw-draw).

The gaps:
1. Parity. Zugzwang, king course, stalemate watch, count-before-you-take and push-or-hold are Learn-only. Review never says them, and real-game endgame moves are never graded by the tablebase.
2. Dual use. The tablebase trainer records nothing. The pawn-ending lane can never record a hold.
3. Arrows (S10). Opposition, basic mates, zugzwang, push-or-hold and chat verdicts carry no squares, and stalemate or spare-tempo lines name moves with no arrow.
4. Duplication. There are 3 tablebase clients, one of them hitting lichess directly without the proxy. The rule-of-the-square arithmetic exists 4 times, isPassed 3 times, knight distance 3 times, and 'attacker' has 2 definitions. Several endgame gates disagree.
5. Teaching depth. The F01 nuggets the brief names are mostly missing: the Réti diagonal, diagonal opposition, outflanking, counting the pawn ending, queening with check. 'Count the pawn ending' tells the student to count but never computes the count. Several hand-authored lessons (Vancura, outflanking, fortress, two weaknesses, stalemate defence) have no live detector, so the coach can teach them in a lesson but never catch them in a game.
MISSING: RÉTI / KING ON THE DIAGONAL: a king walking a diagonal is as fast as a straight line (Chebyshev), so it can chase two goals at once, catching a passer while supporting its own pawn. Example: 7K/8/k1P5/7p/8/8/8/8 w (Réti 1921). Rare (~1 in 1000+ games). Verb: knowledge/consequence. Nothing computes 'one king route serves two targets'.; DIAGONAL OPPOSITION and CORRESPONDING SQUARES: kings on a diagonal with an odd gap, and the pairs of squares the defender must mirror. Example: 8/8/8/2k5/8/4K3/3P4/8. Uncommon in K+P endings. Verb: knowledge.; OUTFLANKING / SHOULDERING: the king steps aside instead of taking the opposition and uses its body to keep the other king off the pawn's path. A lesson exists (pawn-endings 'outflanking'), but there is no detector. Verb: plan.; RULE OF THE SQUARE WITH OBSTACLES: the defending king's path blocked by its own pawns or the enemy king, or the pawn's path blocked. detectRuleOfSquare is K+P vs K only and blockers are ignored. Verb: consequence.; COUNT THE PAWN ENDING: tempo arithmetic (king moves to the target pawn vs pawn moves to queen, plus spare tempi) behind 'count it out before you trade'. Only the engine verdict exists. Verb: consequence/knowledge. Comes up in many amateur endgames.; PAWN RACE EXTRAS: queening WITH CHECK (wins a tempo), a new queen skewering or taking the other new queen, the king blocking its own pawn or standing on the other's queening diagonal, and a king that can catch a passer (rule of the square inside the race). planRace has only pushes plus firstQueenCovers. Verb: consequence.; QUEEN vs PAWN ON THE 7TH: a centre/knight pawn loses, a rook/bishop pawn draws by the stalemate trick unless the attacking king is close. Example: 8/8/8/8/8/2K5/p7/1Q5k. Rare. Verb: knowledge.; ROOK vs PAWN(S): count king and pawn tempi against the rook's checks (does the king get in front in time), and the rule that two connected passers on the 6th beat a rook. Verb: consequence.; VANCURA, SHORT-SIDE / LONG-SIDE checking distance, the 'three-file rule' for frontal checks, and the ACTIVE ROOK: lessons exist (rook-endings vancura-position, active-rook) but there are no detectors. Verb: knowledge/plan.; DEFENSIVE STALEMATE RESOURCE: the LOSING side heads for a stalemate trap (king in the corner, sacrifice the last piece). stalemateWatch only warns the winner. Lesson 'stalemate-stalking' exists. Verb: recognize (both seats).; DON'T TRADE YOUR LAST PAWN / material that can't win: a minor piece up with no pawns cannot win, and K+N or K+B vs K is a draw. detectBareKingMate only fires when one side is already bare. A forward 'if you trade this pawn you can no longer win' rule is missing. Verb: prevent/consequence.; GENERAL FORTRESS: opposite-coloured bishops with the blockade on the bishop's colour, Q vs R+P third-rank fortress, rook-pawn plus wrong bishop beyond K+B+P vs K. Lessons exist (queen-vs-rook-fortress, opposite-color-bishops) and pushOrHold gives advice text only. Verb: recognize/strategize.; OCB WIN CONDITION: does the stronger side have two passers far enough apart, counted in files. pushOrHold says it in prose but does not check it. Verb: plan.; TWO WEAKNESSES principle: a second target on the other wing. Lesson 'two-weaknesses' exists but no computer. Verb: strategize.; PAWN MAJORITY → CANDIDATE PASSER: count a healthy majority, find the candidate pawn, and note who gets a passer first. Verb: plan.; KNIGHT GEOMETRY NUGGETS: a knight changes square colour every move, so it can never lose a tempo or reach a same-colour square in an odd number of moves. A knight is weak against a rook pawn, and a knight can stop a pawn only from in front of it. There are three knight-distance BFS copies but none of these facts. Verb: knowledge.; BISHOP COVERAGE: a bishop covers one colour only (colour complex), and good versus bad bishop in the endgame. This may live in another area; there is no endgame-specific computer. Verb: knowledge.; BISHOP + KNIGHT MATE PROCESS: the live 'W manoeuvre' stage (is the king on the right edge, drive it toward the bishop's corner) with squares. detectBareKingMate only states the rule and the corners. Same for KQ vs K and KR vs K: no 'shrink the box' computer with live squares. Verb: plan.; FIFTY-MOVE COUNT: halfmove clock vs DTZ (a 'cursed win', 'reset the clock with a pawn move'). The tablebase knows DTZ but nothing teaches it. Rare. Verb: knowledge.; TROITZKY LINE (K+2N vs K+P): a blocked pawn behind the Troitzky line means the knights win. Very rare. Verb: knowledge.; PROTECTED / CONNECTED PASSER VALUE: passerKinds computes these, but nothing teaches 'a protected passer ties their king down' or 'connected passers on the 6th beat a rook'. Verb: strategize.

Both have lessons (pawn-endings.json 'breakthrough', 'triangulation') but nothing in a live game ever says them.; tablebases.fetchTablebase (tablebases.ts:88) calls tablebase.lichess.ovh straight from the client instead of /api/lichess-tablebase. ## Deciders and doors: coachDecider, learnTurnDoor, factSelector, reviewFacetRank, voicePackage, playCommentary, teachingSelector, chat routing
This area is where the ONE brain should live, and today there are at least five deciding doors that do not compose. coachDecider.decide (coachDecider.ts:297) is the closest thing to D1: importance, need, board veto, subsumption by coupled squares, a stakes-valued order, and a closing habit, with an audit row on every path. But Learn's live turns go through learnTurnDoor.decideTurn (its own lead table, DNA-beat order, one-thought holds, beginner mode), playCommentary is a single-return ladder, voicePackage keeps its own fixed RANK, teachingSelector runs twice per Review with and without the student, and roughly 63 chat intents voice groundedAnswer facts with no decider at all, behind five-plus routers. Review also re-sorts decide's output and keeps a second need gate because the door's need step is posture-blind.

For F0c the useful seeds are learnTurnDoor.DNA_BEAT (a Learn-only role order), playCommentary's buildRejectedTempting (tempting choice), buildPriorityFirst (remove it), studentMovePoint (reason), slipAnswerText (line), and methodBeat (habit, split into two copies). No computer assigns roles across surfaces.

For S10 the right shape already exists. VoiceFact carries squares and lines, and keptLines/markableSquares draw from the fact. But decide returns plain strings, composePositionRead flattens everything to text, the commentary lane has no squares, three Learn paths re-derive arrows from prose, and voicePackage drops a fact's lines when any one of its sentences is deduped. The plan: one decider with a posture-aware need step, facts that keep their moves and squares all the way to the board, and chat moved onto the same door.
MISSING: F0c ROLE ASSIGNER: nothing labels a fact GOAL / REASON / OBSTACLE / REMOVE-IT / TEMPTING CHOICE / LINE / HABIT. FACT_ROLE is binary teach/describe (reviewFacetRank.ts:267); DNA_BEAT is Learn-only with a different vocabulary (learnTurnDoor.ts:122). The one chain composer cannot be built without it. Serves all verbs; every move.; F05 ASK DECIDER: no shared computer decides when to ASK (long-standing weakness, said why first); each surface withholds on its own (census B3). Serves identify/plan; rare by design.; STUDENT'S OWN PRIOR MOVE HERE (F18 step 2 'what they played last time'): no computer looks up the student's previous game at this position/structure. Example: they played ...Bg4? in this exact Philidor position two games ago. Serves recognize/consequence; frequent for anyone with a repertoire.; LEVEL-MATCHED TEMPTING CANDIDATES (F18 step 2 'what players at their level play'): the amateur explorer cache is read only by the autopilot lane; no computer lists the top human moves at the student's level with why each falls short. Serves consequence; every opening move.; PIN BROKEN WITH CHECK: no computer detects that a pinned piece can leave WITH CHECK, breaking the pin so the pinner hangs (David's first gem: 1.e4 e5 2.Nf3 d6 3.Bc4 Bg4? 4.Bxf7+ Kxf7 5.Ng5+ Ke8 6.Qxg4). grep for unpin/pin-breaks/withCheck finds only a sacrifice-wording flag (coachFeatureService.ts:2741). Serves knowledge/recognize; uncommon but every Legal-style trap.; PERSISTENT TERM LEDGER (V18): first use of a term per student, across games, with understood/not-yet state; today only an in-memory per-game set. Serves knowledge; every new term.; POSTURE-AWARE NEED STEP: decide's need veto ignores posture, so Review cannot hand it need (coachFeatureService.ts:2320-2340). A decider-level computer is missing, not a chess one.; BRIEF SELECTION (V15): Brief clips the first 2 sentences after ordering (applyBriefVoiceCap); in Learn the order is danger→lead→DNA beat before worth (learnTurnDoor.ts:433), so Brief keeps by beat position, not by importance. Nothing picks 'the most important points' for Brief.
BROKEN: DEAD: reviewFacetRank.rankFacets (reviewFacetRank.ts:434) has no callers; facetRank (:378) is reachable only via factSelector's no-`order` fallback, which decide never takes (coachDecider.ts:376).; DEAD: surfaceContract.contractFor and the `withholds`/`speaks` fields have zero readers; SURFACE_CONTRACT has no Play, Tactics or Openings surface (surfaceContract.ts).; DEAD: playCommentary.describeMoveConsequence (playCommentary.ts:368) has no production caller.; DEAD PATH: usePhaseNarration.ts:545-549 builds plies with pliesFromSans(sans) and no evals, so swingPawns returns null (reviewTurningPoint.ts:98) and thesis.kind can never be 'turned' — the phase thesis never speaks. ## Said-once memories (all owners) and the surface register/contract (coach/surfaceContract.ts)
Under the one brain, this area is the coach's short-term memory (D6, V13) and its idea of which surface it is speaking on. Neither works as one system today. About 20 say-once owners hold 70+ sets, keyed by four different "new game" signals: plies going backwards (learnMemory.ts:265), fullmove going backwards (standingFactMemory.ts:57, plus a third copy at coachGameEngine.ts:437), gameId (useLiveCoach.ts:166, CoachGamePage.tsx:3836), and per-call scope (coachFeatureService.ts:1487-1727). The keys come in roughly 12 shapes: normalised prose (factSelector.ts:265 compares w.text, voicePackage.ts:408 sayKey), claim: keys, definitionKey, conceptInstanceKey, forkThreatKey, mustKey, planKey/ideaKey, student-break-sq, and others. So "one fact said once" depends on the wording matching, and the chat, read-position and why-best-move lanes on Learn never see what the live voice already said. Every memory holds strings with no squares. The Review-only refrain fold (standingRefrains, applied as a regex over finished prose) therefore cannot re-highlight the square it refers back to (S10), and Learn has no refrain at all: it suppresses where David asked it to "refer, don't suppress". surfaceContract is half-built. Only registerFor is read (3 sites). contractFor, .withholds and .speaks have zero readers. Play has no surface entry, and its silence is the separate constant PLAY_VOLUNTEERS_COACHING (CoachGamePage.tsx:281). Openings, Tactics, puzzles, How-to-Think and Endgame are absent. Six other register enums are hard-coded at call sites, and the scan gate checks only renderThesis literals. The target is ONE per-game ledger keyed by claim id (fact kind + squares), carrying squares, re-opened by a computed "it changed" verdict, plus ONE persistent per-student term/idea ledger for V18. Every surface reads the register, withholding and speak policy from the contract.
MISSING: ONE claim-identity key per fact: kind + squares (+ seat), emitted by the computer that produced the fact. It replaces the ~12 key shapes and prose matching, so the say-once ledger, subsumption and arrows all read the same id (D6, V13, S10). Verb: other (infrastructure for every verb).; ONE per-game said-ledger, shared by every lane and surface of one game (Learn live + read-position + chat; Review; Play-on-ask; phase transitions). It holds claim ids WITH their squares, so a refrain can re-highlight. One new-game signal (a real game id, which learnMemory already mints at :193).; A 'something changed' computer that RE-OPENS a said fact when its state changes (the pin now also hits the queen; the isolated pawn became a passer; the attacker count changed), as V13 requires ('unless something changed'). Today only a backwards board or a new square instance re-opens anything. Example: Bg5 pins Nf6 to the queen (said); after ...Be7 the pin is relative to nothing, and after Qd2/Bxf6 it changes again. Frequency: most middlegames. Verbs: recognize, consequence.; A persistent per-STUDENT term ledger (V18): pin, outpost, fianchetto, opposition, zugzwang, the square of the pawn… taught in context with an arrow the first time, then the bare word. It needs an 'understood' signal from capabilityEvidence before the explanation stops. Nothing persists today. Verb: knowledge.; A cross-game IDEA memory for spaced repetition of ideas (brief 3d): which concept or nugget was taught when, and when it is due again, keyed by the same claim/concept id. Example: count attackers vs defenders before capturing, taught in game 1 and re-surfaced when it next decides a capture. Verb: knowledge, recognize.; A refrain/refer-back renderer working on structured facts (not regex over prose), available on Learn as well as Review: FULL, then FACT, then REFRAIN, with squares. Verb: identify.; A register-from-surface computer that every tense-bearing line reads (race, causal chain, recurrence, critical moment, hint, posture), so a new surface fails to compile until its register, withholding and speak policy are declared. It needs Play, Openings-play, Tactics, Puzzles, How-to-Think and Endgame added as surfaces.; A withholding computer that reads SURFACE_CONTRACT.withholds (plus F05's 'ask only for a long-standing weakness'). Today 11 sites decide withholding themselves (census §3).; A 'the coach said it would happen and it did / it didn't' follow-through for Review and phase narration. Today learnMemory.promises (learnBoardTeaching.ts:899-913) exists on Learn only. Example: 'their plan is ...f5' announced on move 12 and played on move 15. Verbs: plan, consequence.; A repetition/'we've seen this position before' memory: the same position or structure reached again in the game (threefold looming, or a manoeuvre that returns to the start). Only gameInsightsService.ts and onlyMoveSequence.ts touch threefold; nothing voices it live. Rare, but it decides draws. Verbs: recognize, consequence.; A 'student already found this themselves' memory per game (credited ideas). The coach then refers to the student's own discovery ('the same fork you found on move 9') instead of re-teaching it. Today only the coach's own sayings are remembered. Verbs: recognize, knowledge.
BROKEN: contractFor (surfaceContract.ts:61) has zero production callers. SurfaceContract.withholds and .speaks are read only by surfaceContract.scan.test.ts:41-43. The contract's withholding and speak policy decide nothing.; CoachTeachPage never calls registerFor. Learn's causal chain hard-codes register:'learn' (CoachTeachPage.tsx:10032), an enum that does not exist in SurfaceRegister.; Play has no CoachSurface. Its silence lives in PLAY_VOLUNTEERS_COACHING (CoachGamePage.tsx:281), outside the contract. Openings Play rung, Tactics, Puzzles, How-to-Think and Endgame are not surfaces either, so the 'Record<CoachSurface,…> fails to compile' guarantee covers only 11 of the app's voices.; The scan gate (surfaceContract.scan.test.ts) bans only renderThesis('<literal>'). Six other register enums are hard-coded at call sites and pass it.; Four incompatible 'new game' signals: plies going backwards (learnMemory.ts:265), fullmove going backwards (standingFactMemory.ts:57; coachGameEngine.ts:437 module-global, a third copy of the rule), gameId (useLiveCoach.ts:166; CoachGamePage.tsx:3836), per-call (Review). standingRef is NOT cleared by learnMem.newGame(); it waits for fullmove to drop. After an explicit new-game ask that starts at a fullmove not lower than the last one seen (e.g. a seeded position), Learn forgets learnMemory but keeps standing facts.; Double ledger: takeDefinition (CoachTeachPage.tsx:7752-7758) checks and writes learnMem.conceptTaught AND standingRef.said(definitionKey). positionalSaidRef is unioned into standingRef at :8790 and again at :9742. One fact, three sets.; factSelector said-already compares raw TEXT (factSelector.ts:265), so the same claim in a new wording speaks again. Meanwhile ~12 key shapes coexist in the same set (prose, claim:, definitionKey, conceptInstanceKey, forkThreatKey, mustKey, planKey/ideaKey, student-break-sq, stockKeys, methodKey, tradeTargetKey, convert keys). V13 'one fact once' depends on wording.; learnFundamentalNarration.ts:138 passes seenLabels: new Set() on every call, so the Learn recurrence line has no memory. Review keeps recurrenceLabelsSeen (coachFeatureService.ts:1537).; announcedHangingRef is consumed while Play is silent: buildFastMoveLine adds the square (fastMoveNarration.ts:135), then the line is discarded because PLAY_VOLUNTEERS_COACHING=false (CoachGamePage.tsx:3870). The ledger records warnings never spoken (G8.5 rule 2).; standingRefrains runs only in Review (coachFeatureService.ts:5212). Learn suppresses repeats instead of referring back, contradicting the module's own 'REFER, DO NOT SUPPRESS' rule. It also works by regex over finished prose (standingRefrains.ts:239), so a refrain carries no squares to highlight.; saidHabitsRef is copied verbatim in PuzzleBoard.tsx:183 and MistakePuzzleBoard.tsx:184.; On-request lanes (usePositionNarration, positionReadComposer, whyBestMove, chat) keep no shared memory. On Learn, 'read this position' or a typed question re-states facts the live voice already said this game (D6 violated).; planArc (CoachTeachPage.tsx:1827) and planMemory (planMemory.ts:44) are two plan-identity memories. Review and Openings reach planMemory only through teachingSelector, Learn reaches planArc, so 'the same plan' is decided twice.; No memory survives the game: nothing persistent records which terms or ideas a student has already been taught (V18 says it must).

## The thinking-out-loud chain: thinkAloud.ts, speedRunReads.ts, deliberation.ts, methodBeat.ts (incl. notYet) — what exists, who calls it, what Review lacks
Four modules hold most of the F18 chain's parts, but nothing joins them into one chain. thinkAloud.depthClauses (thinkAloud.ts:226) is the "one producer". It emits typed facts: not-yet, line, their-habit, stop-flaw, hole-access, and 25 speedrun-reads. Its only caller is positionFacts.ts:1009, where the door RANKS those facts beside the deliberation weighing (positionFacts.ts:1342) and the live method beat (positionFacts.ts:839). They are not composed into goal→obstacle→remove-it→line→habit, so F0c and F18 are still unbuilt.

Which surfaces reach it, all through computePositionFacts:
- Learn walk read (CoachTeachPage.tsx:9692)
- phase narration (usePhaseNarration.ts:635)
- the "read this position" tap (positionReadComposer.ts:130)
- the "Why?" tap (whyBestMove.ts:118)
- Play (useLiveCoach.ts:264), which is silenced by PLAY_VOLUNTEERS_COACHING (CoachGamePage.tsx:281)
- Openings walkthrough, which gets only the deliberation alternatives (openingGenerator.ts:2179-2181)

REVIEW GETS ALMOST NONE OF IT. coachFeatureService calls coachDecider directly (coachFeatureService.ts:2370ff). Review hears:
- the retrospective methodBeatFor: 3 habits, and the 'candidates' habit has no branch
- deliberation.moveWhy through turningPoints.ts:262
- refutedAlternative, opening plies only (coachFeatureService.ts:3593)

Review never hears: depthClauses, notYet, line-in-words with what it achieves, opponent habits, the obvious-stop flaw, any of the 25 reads, or the candidate weighing.

Two wiring breaks:
- Learn's main read never passes `history` (CoachTeachPage.tsx:9692-9748 vs positionFacts.ts:1009), so their-habit and finishStarted are dead on Learn.
- Deliberation is off for fullmove < 10 (positionFacts.ts:463,480). The "tempting move and why it fails" therefore never speaks in the opening, which conflicts with F02.

Arrows (S10): many facts drop their squares or moves. Only thinkAloud's 'line' fact carries a full line.
MISSING: A chain composer (F0c/F18): nothing orders the found facts into what-their-move-changed → the candidates you'd consider → why each fails → not-yet/first-this → the line with their reply → what they keep doing → the habit. Today the door ranks flat facts by stakes (coachDecider) and joins them.; Human candidate sourcing for the weighing: deliberation uses only engine top-3 (deliberation.ts:159). Nothing feeds it 'what players at your level play here' (the amateur cache exists, used only by refutedAlternative/autopilot) or 'what YOU played here last time' (no computer found for the student's own previous move at this position). F18 step 2 needs both. Recognize/consequence, every move.; A forcing-move ENUMERATOR the student hears: methodBeat tells them to 'list the checks and captures' (methodBeat.ts:156, :329) but nothing lists them with a one-word verdict each ('Bxf7+ — works; Nxe5 — drops the knight'). Identify, at every deciding moment.; Blunder-check on the move about to be played, prospectively: 'before you play it, what does it allow?' as a per-candidate safety read. Today only post-hoc (safetyHabits.blunderCheck) or engine-gap based. Prevent, often.; 'When to stop calculating' / quiescence: recognising that a line ends in a quiet position you can evaluate (no checks, captures or threats left). Nothing names the stopping point of a line. Knowledge, at sharp moments.; Breaking-the-pin-with-check (the brief's first gem nugget): a pin only holds while the pinned piece can't move WITH CHECK. obviousStopFlaw sees only bishop pins on a knight, and no computer states the pin's failure condition. Knowledge, rare-to-common in openings (Légal/Bxf7+ Ng5+ patterns).; Zwischenzug-as-not-yet in Review: notYet/rejectedMoveLater exist only on live surfaces. Review has no 'you took at once; the in-between move came first' explanation for a played capture. Consequence, common at club level.; Opponent's recurring plan failure across the game beyond tempo and undeveloped minors (F18 step 5): e.g. repeatedly ignoring king safety, repeatedly trading into worse endings, repeatedly leaving a piece loose. opponentHabits has only 2 kinds (thinkAloud.ts:180-188). Recognize, frequent.; Clock/decision triage tied to the record: anyMoveFine/usefulWaiting say 'save time' from the engine spread only. Nothing reads the student's own time use (no clock data in this chain). Strategize, every game.; 'Take with the right piece': which recapture keeps structure or development (exists as recaptureChoice in Learn). Not linked into the weighing, so a tempting recapture is never ruled out with that reason. Plan, common.
BROKEN: Learn's main read never passes `history` to computePositionFacts (CoachTeachPage.tsx:9692-9748; positionFacts.ts:1009 uses `input.history ?? []`). So opponentHabits/their-habit (thinkAloud.ts:239) and finishStarted (via ownLastMove, thinkAloud.ts:216/257) never fire on Learn.; usePhaseNarration (usePhaseNarration.ts:635-656), positionReadComposer (:130-147) and whyBestMove (:118) pass no opponentLastMove, so speedRunReads.positionOpened can never fire there. useLiveCoach passes neither history nor opponentLastMove (useLiveCoach.ts:264-290), and Play is muted anyway (CoachGamePage.tsx:281).; MethodHabit 'candidates' (methodBeat.ts:60) is computed into habit standing via STEP_METHOD_HABIT (thinkingSteps.ts:168, coachDecider.ts:498), but methodBeatFor has no 'candidates' branch (methodBeat.ts:116-229). It is a dead union member, so Review never teaches candidate discipline.; Deliberation is gated off for fullmove < 10 (positionFacts.ts:463,480). The tempting-choice weighing therefore never speaks in the opening (e.g. 3...Bg4? Bxf7+), against F02/F04.; Squares or moves are dropped before reaching arrows (S10): the deliberation clause has no squares or lines (positionFacts.ts:1342) though it names candidates and proof lines; not-yet drops its capture (thinkAloud.ts:274); stop-flaw drops threatSan/stopSan (:252); their-habit drops tempoCount's squares (:183/:240); the idea form drops squares (:258).; speedRunReads that name moves with no squares field: anyMoveFine :77, uglyButRight :103, castleSide :119, playAnyway :289, bestCasePlan :455, rejectedMoveLater :505, flexibleFirst :560. No read carries `lines`, so named moves get at most a highlight, never an arrow.; thinkAloud.sayLine default maxPlies=4 (thinkAloud.ts:114) truncates the spoken and arrowed line. This is a cap on a computed fact (G4.5) and conflicts with S10 'a line is arrowed in full'.; positionFacts.ts:1018 keeps a hand-written rank ladder for the depth kinds (not-yet 90, stop-flaw 70, line 60, hole-access 50, speedrun-read 45). That contradicts 'no per-surface rank table'. Whether the door's value ordering fully overrides it was not verified.; Dual-use gap: the depth kinds (not-yet, line, their-habit, stop-flaw, hole-access, 16 of 25 speedrun reads) have no lane in COMPUTER_ROLES (computerRoles.ts:44ff) and no record path. moveInsight.positionPosed (moveInsight.ts:754-761) records only 9 reads as posed.; Name collisions that invite wrong imports: thinkAloud.sayLine vs spokenMove.sayLine (spokenMove.ts:97); deliberation.moveWhy vs the private reviewOpeningTheory.moveWhy (reviewOpeningTheory.ts:573) vs the moveWhy.ts module.; Duplicate habit lanes for one fact: learnBoardTeaching.tempoTeaching (:348, lane 'tempo') and thinkAloud.opponentHabits both speak tempoCount. No shared claim key was found (claims `tempo-count:` vs SAY_ONCE kind 'their-habit').

## Engine reads: stockfishEngine singleton, worker pool, caches, every call-site budget
The one brain needs one engine read, and today it has two engines that read the board differently. The singleton (`stockfishEngine.ts`) gives 3 lines plus WDL, seldepth and bounds. The pool (`gameAnalysisService.ts:420-958`) is pinned to 1 line, has no WDL, cuts its PV at 8 plies, and its adapter turns a mate into mate:null. The same fact (a close alternative, whether the position is contested, how far away mate is) therefore depends on which engine ran.

There are three caches that do not agree:
- Cache A is keyed on full FEN and requested depth.
- Cache B ignores depth and is used by 8 hooks/services (the census said 2).
- Cache C is persistent Dexie, read only by the batch analysis.

Three correctness bugs matter most for an honest chain:
1. **MultiPV leaks.** Per-call {MultiPV:N} options persist on the shared worker. A puzzle solve leaves it at 1 or 2 lines, which makes scanCriticality call positions only-moves and disables detectEnginePunish's uniqueness check.
2. **Shallow reads cached as deep.** Budget-stopped searches are cached under the depth that was requested, and the cache serves deeper keys to shallower asks.
3. **Abandoned searches keep running.** Several races stop waiting but leave the engine searching.

Most line computers carry UCI moves, so arrows are possible (S10). The exception is criticalMoment's proofs, which exist only as prose.

**Recommended shape for the one brain:** one engine read keyed on (4-field FEN, depth reached, MultiPV), shared by the singleton and the pool, with every option set and restored per search, and every fact built from it carrying its moves. The missing computers are teachable engine facts nobody says yet: WDL in words, shallow-vs-deep traps, use-it-or-lose-it, the human-likely reply, and which side is easier to play.
MISSING: WDL AS A TAUGHT FACT. Stockfish already sends win/draw/loss on every singleton line (stockfishEngine.ts:2122-2142), but it is used only as a gate (narrationImportance.ts:138; positionFacts.ts:707) and never said. Detects: how often each side scores from here ('you hold this about three times in four'), and positions where the cp score is large but most results are draws (opposite bishops, wrong-colour rook pawn). Example: KB+RP vs K with the wrong bishop, +5 cp but almost all draws. Frequency: every endgame and most quiet middlegames. Verb: CONSEQUENCE/KNOWLEDGE.; DISCOVERY DEPTH / SHALLOW-vs-DEEP DISAGREEMENT. The depth at which the best move first becomes best, and whether a shallow search prefers a move the deep search refutes. This is the honest 'looks good at a glance, fails two moves later' signal. criticality.ts:111-114 uses a seldepth spike as a stand-in, and nothing records per-depth best moves. Example: a Greek-gift Bxh7+ that a 6-ply search likes and a 16-ply search refutes. Frequency: about 1 in 10 critical moments. Verb: RECOGNIZE/CONSEQUENCE (the tempting choice and why it fails, F18 step 2).; USE-IT-OR-LOSE-IT (DYNAMIC vs STATIC EDGE). Compare the eval now with the eval after the student makes a quiet waiting move, to measure whether an advantage is temporary (initiative, development lead) and must be cashed now. Example: an open e-file with the enemy king uncastled, +1.2 now but +0.2 if you play h3. Frequency: common in openings at club level. Verb: STRATEGIZE.; HUMAN-PLAUSIBLE REPLY in the line. Engine lines give the best reply; nothing picks the reply a player at the student's level is likely to play past the opening explorer. F18 step 4 ('the line with their reply in words') and the trap definition (F04) both need it. Example: the engine shows ...Kxf7 forced while most club players try ...Ke7. Frequency: every line spoken in a middlegame. Verb: CONSEQUENCE/PREVENT.; WHICH SIDE IS EASIER TO PLAY. Aggregate readCriticalMoment counts over the next N plies of the best line for each side ('they need the only move four times in the next six; you have several good moves each time'). Today it is computed per ply only (criticalMoment.ts:164). Example: an equal-eval position where the defender must find successive only moves. Frequency: about 1 in 20 positions. Verb: STRATEGIZE.; ENGINE-CONFIRMED FORTRESS / NO PROGRESS. The eval stays high across depth and across the best line's plies while no pawn moves or captures happen and WDL is mostly draws. conceptEngine and pushOrHold mention fortresses, but nothing engine-verifies one. Example: R vs R+B with the defending king in the right corner. Frequency: rare, about 1 game in 200. Verb: KNOWLEDGE/PLAN.; 50-MOVE / CURSED-WIN TEACHING. Tablebase categories include cursed-win and blessed-loss (tablebases.ts:4) and DTZ is fetched, but nothing teaches 'this is a win but you cannot convert it in time' or 'you must reset the counter with a pawn move'. Example: KBN vs K started late in the 50-move count. Frequency: about 1 in 1000 games. Verb: KNOWLEDGE (technique and rules).; MANOEUVRE READ OFF THE ENGINE LINE. A piece the PV moves two or more times toward one square ('the engine's idea is Nd2-f1-g3: the knight wants g3') as a plan fact carrying its route squares for arrows. Check against nextPlans and pieceRoutes before building. Example: a Ruy Lopez knight reroute. Frequency: common in closed middlegames. Verb: PLAN.; PERSISTENT, TRANSPOSITION-AWARE READS FOR LIVE SURFACES (infrastructure that enables speed, brief item 3g). This is not a teaching fact: one cache keyed on the 4-field FEN, depth, MultiPV and the depth actually reached, shared by the singleton and the pool.
BROKEN: DEAD: stockfishEngine.evaluateMove (stockfishEngine.ts:2026) has 0 production callers. It also drops mate scores (it parses only `score cp`, :2034) and sends position/go outside the pending/mutex machinery.; BROKEN (MultiPV leak): per-call {MultiPV:N} options persist on the singleton. _dispatchAnalysis resets only Skill Level and UCI_LimitStrength (stockfishEngine.ts:1612-1627); nothing resets MultiPV. Leakers: mistakePuzzleService.ts:1459, reached through mistakeLineGrowth.ts:77 (multiPV 1) and :85 (multiPV 2) after every clean puzzle solve (:1425); and coachAnswerGates.ts:400 (MultiPV 5). Later reads are cached under fen::depth with the wrong fan width. The consequences: scanCriticality sets gapCp=Infinity and severity 'only-move' when the runner-up is missing (criticalityScan.ts:122-127); detectEnginePunish skips its uniqueness gate (engineDeltaLines.ts:165); SINGLETON_SCORER can return fewer scored moves than it was asked for (refutedAlternative.ts:59-62); computePvLine.closeAlternative disappears.; BROKEN (shallow result cached as deep): _dispatchAnalysis sets cacheDepth to the REQUESTED depth (stockfishEngine.ts:1568-1569), and the bestmove handler caches it whatever analysis.depth was actually reached (:~2180). A budget-stopped analyzeWithBudget read (900ms in CoachTeachPage :2857/:10797/:10828/:10925) or a movetime-capped asm/single search (SEARCH_BUDGET_MS :316-319) is therefore stored as d14/d16. stockfishCache.get also serves a DEEPER key to a shallower ask (stockfishCache.ts:63-67), so a d12 caller can receive a 'd14' that really reached d8. In-flight joining has the same flaw (stockfishCache.ts:99-116). holdForQuestion is the only path that un-caches a cut-short search (:1866-1869).; BROKEN (cache B ignores depth): getCachedStockfish(fen) has no depth argument (stockfishFenCache.ts:22). enginePlanContext's PLAN_DEPTH=18 plan and alternatives (:31, :68, :169) can be answered by a d10 narration read or a d12 ponder read.; BROKEN (unbounded search, the defect the same file already fixed at :97): buildAlternativesContext calls analyzePosition(fen, 18) with no budget (enginePlanContext.ts:172).; BROKEN (a race abandons the waiter but leaves the engine searching, so the next read queues behind a result nobody will use): CoachGamePage.tsx:3183-3184 (withTimeout 5s), coachContextEnricher.ts:367, thinkingCandidatesStep.ts:46-47, coachGameEngine.ts:540 (withBudget on an unbudgeted analyzePosition).; RACE: setMultiPv is sent immediately (stockfishEngine.ts:2015), but the analyzeWithBudget it is meant for may wait on _brainMutex. CoachGameReview.tsx:933/:937 (8 then 3) and CoachTeachPage.tsx:9424/:9427 (6 then 3) therefore widen whichever search runs next and can narrow before their own search starts. Widening is also skipped on a cache hit: analyzeWithBudget returns the cached 3-line read first (:1903).; PLAUSIBLE: getBestMove (stockfishEngine.ts:1723-1810) does not register this.pending and does not take _brainMutex. It can send position/go while an analysis is in flight, and handleMessage (:2091) would accept that worker's bestmove line as the pending analysis's own result. The same bypass exists in evalBoard (:2049), which sends `position fen` while a search may be running.; CONTRACT SPLIT, pool vs singleton: the pool never sets UCI_ShowWDL and pins MultiPV 1 (gameAnalysisService.ts:772); its PvEngine adapter returns one line with mate:null and mateIn:null (:940-951); its PV is cut to 8 plies (:485). Facts computed on the pool therefore lack WDL (narrationImportance.ts:138's contested gate), lack closeAlternative, and lack signed mate distance.; KEY MISMATCH: cache A keys on the full FEN including move counters (stockfishCache.ts:33), while cache C keys on the 4-field FEN (positionEvalCache.ts:48). The same position at a different move number, or reached by transposition, misses cache A. The singleton never consults cache C at all, so Learn, Play and chat re-search positions the batch analysis has already stored.; DUPLICATE CLIENTS: three tablebase clients. tablebases.ts:88 hits tablebase.lichess.ovh directly, bypassing the /api/lichess-tablebase proxy that lichessTablebaseService.ts:19 and endgameTablebaseService.ts:24 use. Each defines its own countPieces and its own TablebaseCategory type.; STALE DOCS: stockfishEval.ts:3 says it wraps queueAnalysis but calls analyzeWithBudget (:39). The queueAnalysis doc (stockfishEngine.ts:1810-1814) says analyzePosition cancels in-flight work, which brain reads no longer do (_brainMutex :1485). analyzePosition's comment (:1394-1395) says 'today only the brain and prefetch path call without options', but there are now about 90 call sites.; S10 gap: readCriticalMoment's discardedProofs carry the refutation only as prose text ({san, text}, criticalMoment.ts:85), with no move list, so the line that proves a decoy fails cannot be arrowed in full from the fact.; Census correction: cache B has 8 users, not 2 (writers: usePositionNarration, usePhaseNarration, useHintSystem, useEnginePonder; readers: useLiveCoach, GameChatPanel, enginePlanContext, coachContextEnricher). The census also omits a third, persistent cache (positionEvalCache) and the three tablebase clients.

## Arrows and highlights: the arrow door and every resolver; why only 7 of 64 plies were arrowed
This area only shows things. It decides nothing. It has one strong door, `arrowDoor.admitArrows` (src/services/arrowDoor.ts:187). The arrowDoor.gate test enforces that every coach arrow goes through it. In front of the door sit about 9 resolvers, and most of them re-derive moves from the PROSE: namedMoveArrows, deriveNarrationArrows, injectCandidateArrows, spokenLineArrows, extractMoveArrows, moveOrderArrows, VoiceChatMic's [ARROW:] tags, plus two square scrapers. There are also 4 dedupers and 3 colour tables. Only a few facts carry their own moves and squares: TeachingHint.arrows/squares, VoiceFact.squares/lines, ClauseItem.lines, Review keySquares/spokenLineArrows, lineCalc.lineArrows and the Learn gem reveal.

Why plies go unarrowed (traced in code):
1. Learn's instant wave draws only alert arrows plus `leadEyeArrows`, which is never filled (CoachTeachPage.tsx:7972). Named moves get arrows only in the late queue (queueSpokenHint, :9028).
2. Late arrows are dropped when the board moved before they landed (:11184/:11208), or when a fact's queued line can't be matched by claim or text (:11198).
3. 23 TeachingHint lanes ship `arrows: []`.
4. Lines are cut short:
   - winning, mate and movePoint lines use the play/theirs roles with no `vouchedBy`, so any sacrifice is refused as unsafe;
   - GemCrush carries only the first punish move;
   - Review keeps one line per segment, and only the first ply when nothing is proven;
   - caps: MAX_CANDIDATE_ARROWS 3, MAX_GREEN_ARROWS_PER_PLY 4 (above it, zero), moveOrderArrows 8, highlights 3.
5. The door cannot draw x-ray lines (pin, skewer, battery) or quiet threats.

Under the one chain, the fix is that each role-fact (goal, obstacle, remove-it, tempting choice, line) carries typed {moves, squares, lines}. One adapter then turns those into door claims, and no resolver reads prose. Highlights need a door of their own.
MISSING: X-RAY / THROUGH-PIECE LINE role at the door: pin (attacker → pinned piece → what is behind it), skewer, battery, discovered attack, x-ray defence. Detects: a line piece whose ray passes one blocker to a valuable target. Example: Bg4 pinning Nf3 to Qd1. Frequency: in most middlegames. Verb: recognize (V18 term arrows).; QUIET-THREAT arrow: the opponent's next move that wins material or mates without capturing or checking now (pawn-fork push, piece-trapping move, Qh5 eyeing f7). Example: …e5 forking Bd4/Nf4. Frequency: frequent in amateur games. Verb: prevent.; TEMPTING-CHOICE visual for F18: a distinct non-'play this' mark (highlight-only or dashed) for the move the student would consider plus the square where it fails, so the 'why it fails' beat is on the board without ever being a green arrow. Example: 'Qxb2? Then Rb1 traps the queen'. Frequency: every critical moment. Verb: consequence.; PIECE ROUTE / MANEUVER computer with multi-hop arrows for COMPUTED plans (not only authored lessons): Nd2–f1–g3, king route to a weak pawn (kingCourse names a target with no arrow), rook lift. Frequency: every quiet middlegame and endgame. Verb: plan.; LINE ORDER numbering: a multi-ply line on one static board needs per-ply order (moveOrderArrows computes `order` but BoardArrow has no field for it, so 8 arrows on one board become an unreadable diagram). Verb: consequence.; RULE-OF-THE-SQUARE region highlight (the pawn's square; can the king catch it). Example: K+P race. Frequency: about 1 in 20 endgames. Verb: knowledge (geometry nugget).; OPPOSITION / KEY SQUARES highlight in K+P endings (direct, distant, diagonal; key squares in front of the pawn). Frequency: K+P endgames. Verb: knowledge.; KNIGHT REACH / COLOUR-COMPLEX highlight: squares a knight covers in N moves; the bishop's colour complex and the weak squares of the other colour. Verb: knowledge (geometry).; COUNT-BEFORE-YOU-TAKE visual: every attacker and defender of the contested square, by side (countMethod returns one square only). Verb: knowledge (arithmetic nugget).; KING FLIGHT / LUFT squares highlight for back-rank and mating nets (which escape squares are missing and why). Verb: identify.; OVERLOADED-DEFENDER visual: the defender plus its two duties as rays. Verb: recognize.; PAWN-RACE visual: both promotion paths with push counts and who has the move (planRace counts pushes but draws nothing). Verb: knowledge (whoever has the move wins the tie).; KING-WALKS-A-DIAGONAL dual-goal path: one king path serving two targets (Réti-style). Rare; Verb: knowledge.; PAWN-BREAK lever highlight: the break square and the pawn it attacks, for the plan step (in the 2-break vs 1-break case). Verb: plan.; OUTPOST / HOLE semantics in colour: today every key square is yellow. Target vs outpost vs hole vs weakness cannot be told apart, so 'the d5 outpost' and 'their weak c6 pawn' look the same. Verb: identify.; HIGHLIGHT DOOR: one admission check for highlights (the square holds the piece or emptiness the words claim; a role for each colour; no highlight without words), mirroring arrowDoor. Verb: other (infrastructure).; ARROW COVERAGE EMITTER: per spoken line, named moves vs arrows drawn, wordless arrows, and refusals with reasons, emitted through one audit row so 'named moves arrowed 100%, wordless 0' is a measured contract rather than a hand count. Verb: other (instrument).; ONE FACT→CLAIM ADAPTER: each chain role-fact (goal, obstacle, remove-it, tempting choice, line, habit) carries typed {moves, squares, lines, role}, and one function turns it into door claims and highlights. This replaces the ~9 prose resolvers. Verb: other (the root fix in brief item 4).
BROKEN: DEAD: CoachTeachPage `leadEyeArrows` (CoachTeachPage.tsx:7972) is declared and returned (:8946) but nothing ever pushes to it, so the instant-wave arrow branch at :10313 never fires. The instant wave therefore draws only alertArrow (threat/hang). Named moves in instant-wave facts NEVER get arrows; namedMoveArrows runs only in the late queue (:9028). This is a primary cause of 7/64.; DEAD: arrowEngine.computeLeadEyeArrows / computeThreatArrow / computeLookAheadArrow (arrowEngine.ts:104/61/89) have no caller outside the file.; DEAD/G0 hole: VoiceChatMic.extractArrows (VoiceChatMic.tsx:32) parses model-written [ARROW:] tags. No prompt asks for them, so it is either dead or a leak path for model-chosen arrows.; G6 violation still in a prompt: CoachGameReview.tsx:195 tells the model to 'Use a [BOARD: arrow:from-to:green] marker to show the move'.; Learn late arrows are DROPPED SILENTLY when the board moved before the package landed: `liveFenRef.current === fenAfterReply` guards at CoachTeachPage.tsx:11184, :11202, :11208, :11242, :11246.; Learn late arrows are lost when a kept fact cannot find its queued line: matched by claims[0], otherwise by text or text.includes (CoachTeachPage.tsx:11197-11199). Any rephrasing or trimming of the fact text breaks the match (PLAUSIBLE).; 23 TeachingHint lanes return `arrows: []` (learnBoardTeaching.ts), e.g. kingCourseTeaching :860, countMethodTeaching :410, splitPositionTeaching :399, checkMethodTeaching. They depend on prose re-derivation, which draws nothing when the move is named in words ('the king heads for the weak pawn').; Lines broken by the safety check: winningLine/mateArrows/movePoint (learnBoardTeaching.ts:710,719,172-173) use role play/theirs with NO vouchedBy, so a sacrifice ply (Bxf7+ Kxf7 Ng5+…) is refused 'unsafe' (arrowDoor.ts:166-168). The line is drawn with a hole. They should use role 'line', which skips the SEE check.; Gem lines arrowed on the first move only: computeGemCrush returns one red inaccuracy arrow plus the FIRST punish move (gemCrushLines.ts:370-373); punishSeq is never arrowed. Review then admits it as a single 'play' (coachFeatureService.ts:2972). David's own first gem (Bxf7+ Kxf7 Ng5+ Ke8 Qxg4) would show only Bxf7.; Review keeps ONE spoken line per segment (attachLineArrows priority, coachFeatureService.ts:3745) and only 1 ply when proofCut finds nothing (:3737). At :4178 it attaches line arrows even when the rendered `tail` is empty, which gives a wordless arrow. The plies drawn come from the full line while the spoken tail is line.slice(1), so voice and board can disagree (PLAUSIBLE).; Review planArrows is a single slot: causal OR plan OR threat (coachFeatureService.ts:3385-3387). The other computed arrow sets are thrown away, which is computed-and-dropped under G8.5.; Caps that delete named arrows/highlights (S10 + G4.5 + David 2026-08-10 'remove all caps'): MAX_CANDIDATE_ARROWS=3 (arrowEngine.ts:433, used at :398,:404); MAX_CANDIDATE_HIGHLIGHTS=3 (:435, openingGenerator.ts:2415); coachMoveExtractor DEFAULT_MAX=3 (:34); moveOrderArrows maxPlies=8 (:55); MAX_GREEN_ARROWS_PER_PLY=4 → ZERO arrows for a line longer than 4 (openingGenerator.ts:1296,1320).; Chat drops every named move outside the engine top 3 that is not forcing (colorForRank null, arrowEngine.ts:305-309,394-397). Chat also resolves each SAN on the same static board, so a recited line beyond its first move is lost or wrongly placed (resolveSanToArrow :246).; Wrong-colour arrow on a bad move: trapAheadTeaching draws the trap slip as role 'missed', which arrowDoor.colourFor (:172) paints GREEN, i.e. 'play this'. Its own comment says 'natural move in red'. Its punishing-line arrows are also not spoken: the text names only the move and the frequency (:562), so these are arrows without words.; Arrows on moves the lesson says to avoid: deriveNarrationArrows has no ruled-out filter. 'avoid/never/not X' only marks an alternative (narrationArrows.ts:77). narrationArrowsThroughDoor vouches 'book', so the door's safety check is lifted (arrowDoor.ts:163-164). Result: a green play arrow on the avoided move across every Openings lesson surface (PLAUSIBLE; no tape example in hand).; namedMoveArrows vouches 'engine' for ANY prose-named single move (learnBoardTeaching.ts:494), so the safety check is lifted for moves the engine never chose.; Capability-parity gap: Learn passes causalChainHighlights but not causalChainArrows (CoachTeachPage.tsx:10041); Review passes causalChainArrows but not highlights (coachFeatureService.ts:1878).; spokenSquares is written only by useLiveCoach (Play, :305). Learn's narration never records its squares, so Learn's 'show me' (CoachTeachPage.tsx:3499) reads stale or empty data.; No highlight door: highlights are validated only by a square regex. Nothing checks that a highlighted square holds what the words say, and nothing enforces 'no highlight without words' symmetrically with arrows.; Comment/colour drift: arrowEngine colorForRank comment says yellow for ranks 2-3 while the door shades blue (arrowDoor.ts:87); the Review devTargets comment says 'Blue = student, amber = opponent' (coachFeatureService.ts:4489) while the door draws green/red.; Door cannot express x-ray lines: role 'vision' requires a clear path (arrowDoor.ts:101-122), so a pin line through the pinned piece, a skewer, a battery or a discovered attack is refused 'no-sight'. V18 ('a pin draws the line through the pinned piece') is unbuildable today.; Door cannot draw a QUIET threat: role 'threat' needs material won now or check (arrowDoor.ts:170-171). A pawn push that forks, a trapping move or a quiet mate threat is refused 'wins-nothing'.; No instrument measures the brief's metric (named moves arrowed %, wordless arrows count). The arrow door's `refused` list is returned but never emitted to the audit stream (arrowDoor.ts:187-200), so a dropped arrow is invisible.

## Phrasing/voice helpers: piece names, SAN-to-words, list joining, perspective, sideWord, principle tables
This area is the rendering layer, and every computer in the one thinking chain passes through it. It is the most duplicated layer in the app. There is one canonical piece-name table (types/tacticTypes.PIECE_NAMES, 22 importers) beside about 107 inline copies, and about 15 reverse word-to-letter tables. Eight SAN-to-words renderers say the same move differently: Learn dictation's 'Rook a to d 8', the TTS chokepoint's 'a-rook to d8' and spokenMove's 'the rook from a8'. spokenMove claims to be the one renderer, and it also drops check and mate. There are 18 inline list joins beside andList, more than six number-word tables, four eval wordings, and four principle ID spaces with five-plus prose tables, so the same habit has two or three different sentences. Perspective has no single seat function. At least 12 side helpers disagree, and live paths still say 'White is threatening' (engineDeltaLines), 'White castles' (Tactics Create) or 'I / my' (perspectiveRule's coach-is-opponent mode, against V1/V2). opponentVoice is dead. The most important finding for the brain concerns S10: every renderer here returns a bare string and throws away the squares and moves it names, and three systems (structureProse, seatPieces and the SAN tokenizers) re-derive facts from finished sentences. For the one composer, this layer must become a small set of typed renderers: piece, pawn, line, move-with-consequence and seat word. Each should take a fact that carries its squares and seat, and return the words plus the marks, so arrows come from the fact and never from the sentence. The F01 knowledge naming a coach uses (light-squared bishop, long diagonal, rule of the square) has no phrase builders at all.
MISSING: PIECE IDENTITY NAMER: 'your light-squared bishop', 'their good/bad bishop', 'your king's knight', 'the queen's rook'. Coaches name pieces by role and colour complex; nothing computes it. Square-colour code exists four times (positionReadingService:455, nextPlans:34, endgameMatchup:74, bishopGameUtils:20) but no phrase builder. Every middlegame (common). Verb: identify/strategize.; PAWN NAMER: 'your c-pawn', 'the rook pawn', 'the candidate pawn', 'the backward d-pawn', with its role attached. File naming is inline in about 13 places with no shared helper. Common. Verb: identify.; LINE/DIAGONAL NAMER: 'the long diagonal', 'the a2-g8 diagonal', 'the seventh rank', 'the half-open c-file'. No diagonalName/rankName helper exists (grep finds 0); 'long diagonal' is typed by hand in 9 places. Common. Verb: knowledge/identify.; OPPONENT REFERENCE CHOOSER (V2): rotates 'they' / 'your opponent' / 'that bishop on b4', keyed and deterministic. Today every template hardcodes they/their. Every line. Verb: other (voice).; TERM GLOSSARY WITH BOARD GEOMETRY (V18): one definition per term (pin, outpost, fianchetto, opposition), plus the squares and line to arrow on the current board, plus a per-student 'taught' flag. Definitions are scattered across tactic and concept tables. Common. Verb: knowledge.; SEAT-TYPED FACT RENDERING: a renderer that takes {owner: student|opponent|none, piece, square} and produces the words, instead of seatPieceReferences rewriting finished sentences. Every line. Verb: other.; MOVE-WITH-CONSEQUENCE NOUN: says a move together with what it does in one phrase ('the check on b5 that drops the rook') and keeps its from/to squares for the arrow (S10). Today the SAN renderers return bare strings. Common. Verb: consequence.; GEOMETRY NUGGETS AS SPEAKABLE FACTS (F01 knowledge, rare to occasional): 'the rule of the square' with the square's corners named; 'a king walks a diagonal as fast as a straight line' (Reti-style double goals); 'the wrong-coloured rook pawn' (bishop plus rook pawn draws); 'knight on the rim covers only 2-4 squares' with the count. These need phrase builders bound to the computers that detect them; rare (perhaps 1 game in 50-1000).; WHOSE-MOVE CHECK/MATE WORDING: a consistent spoken suffix ('with check', 'and it's mate') carried on every rendered move. spokenMove currently drops it.
BROKEN: DEAD: opponentVoice.speakAsOpponent / framedOpponentPlan (services/opponentVoice.ts:108/203). The only importer is its test, and it contradicts V1/V2 (first-person 'I').; RULE CONFLICT: perspectiveRule 'coach-is-opponent' mode (perspectiveRule.ts:72-75) tells the model to say 'I / my'. It is live via envelope.ts:449, against V1/V2 ('never I').; PERSPECTIVE: tacticNarrationService.describeMove (:12), reached via createReplayNarration (TacticCreatePage.tsx:137), replays the student's own game as 'White castles kingside', breaking V1/V2.; PERSPECTIVE: engineDeltaLines.computeThreatDelta (:55-58) says 'And now White is threatening …' to a seated student, in Learn walkthrough (useTeachWalkthrough.ts:1259), Openings Play (OpeningPlayMode.tsx:479) and continuationMoveNarration.ts:147 (V2: no bare colour). gemCrushLines.ts:776 says 'White crushes with' in the same way.; PERSPECTIVE: thinkingAssessStep.sideWord (:52) chooses Your/Their by side to move, not by the student's seat.; S10: useTeachWalkthrough.ts:171/178 says 'The actual punishment was <Queen takes g4>' for a move that is not played on the board, with arrows: [] or none. The SAN is pre-expanded, so no resolver can recover the arrow.; LOST FACT: spokenMove.sayMoveClause/sayMoveNoun strip +/# (spokenMove.ts:69/83), so the spoken line drops 'check' and 'mate' for every one of its 14 callers.; INCONSISTENT SPOKEN FORM: sanToSpeech says 'Rook a to d 8' / 'e takes d 5' / 'Castle kingside'; voiceService says 'a-rook to d8' / 'e-pawn takes d5' / 'castle kingside'; spokenMove says 'the rook from a8' / 'castle short'. voiceService.ts:510-517 calls sanToSpeech's form robotic, yet Learn dictation (PlayableLinePlayer) still uses it.; V8: countWords (utils/countWords.ts:16) speaks 'N points' for 3 or more; gemCrushLines.ts:786 speaks '${share}% of club players'; data/principles.ts:23 says 'about three moves'.; V14 CAP: lookaheadPlan.ts:1075 `idle.slice(0, 3)` names only 3 idle pieces however many are idle.; WEAK TYPE: methodBeat.FUNDAMENTAL_HABIT is Record<string,…> (:64), so a renamed FundamentalId silently drops the habit bridge.; UNUSED GATE: perspectiveRule.BANNED_PRONOUNS is applied only in a test against data; no production check runs it on code-template output.; PROSE SCRAPING: structureProse.parseStructureAtom (:27) regex-parses facts back out of describeStructure's strings. That is the anti-pattern G4.5.1 names, and it loses the squares.; CENSUS CORRECTIONS: there are ~107 piece-name table copies, not ~40. There are 18 inline list joins. coachFeatureService:5328 CALLS renderFundamentalsRecap; it is not a duplicate.; Math.random in a teaching-pool pick: adaptiveEndgameService.ts:337 (V12 wants rotation, not random; check whether this is selection or phrasing).

## Learn surface end to end: CoachTeachPage (src/components/Coach/CoachTeachPage.tsx, 15,326 lines) and its lanes. It covers what the page computes per move, how it decides, what it remembers, what it says and draws, and what it records.
Learn is the richest surface in the app, with 50 lanes declared in learnTurnDoor.LEARN_LANES, each fed by a real computer. Its gaps are about shape, not raw capability. Every lane emits its own sentence. Three deciders touch one turn (decideTurn three times, plus coachDecider inside the positionFacts lane), and the speed-run thinking chain (thinkAloud + speedRunReads) is buried as one lane among 50. So F0c (one chain of goal → obstacle → remove-it → line → habit) does not exist yet.

The highest-value break is the gem path. On a named opening the coach returns book moves before the taught-slip lane runs, and slipsAllowed blocks gems for anyone rated 1000 or higher on Medium. That explains why David has never heard a gem in Learn.

Other root problems:
- Three different cost computations for one student move. The record (discussion practice) uses a different number from the voice, which breaks G7, D7 and F13.
- Arrows are re-derived from the words (namedMoveArrows) instead of travelling on the fact. A dozen lanes carry no squares or no line arrows.
- Rating still decides how much is said: causal-chain depth, the tactic look-ahead, beginner mode and the hint-dial seed.
- The grade and the gem resolution speak outside the door.
- Remaining caps: rejected-tempting at 2 per game, and concept max:1.
- Small violations of V1/V8: 'I let you off there' and '% of club players'.

For the one brain, Learn's lanes are the natural catalogue of roles. Most already carry squares, claims and stakes and can map onto F0c roles directly. The missing computers listed here, starting with 'a pin broken by a move with check', are trap-tier and should feed the one trap library.
MISSING: PIN BROKEN WITH CHECK (David's first gem: 3…Bg4? 4.Bxf7+ Kxf7 5.Ng5+ Ke8 6.Qxg4). A relative pin only holds while the pinned piece cannot leave WITH CHECK, or with a bigger threat. If it can, the pin is an illusion and the pinner hangs. pinGeometry.isRealPin (pinGeometry.ts:135-165) checks value behind > front, canLeaveLine, capture of the pinner and a hanging front piece. It never asks whether the front piece can move with check or mate threat. Verbs: recognize / consequence. Frequency: common in opening traps (Légal-type and Bg4-pin traps), so trap-tier.; Zwischenzug, deflection, decoy/attraction, clearance and interference NAMED LIVE. They are absent from TacticPatternType, so Learn's instant alert cannot name them. Decoy/attraction, desperado and windmill exist in neither vocabulary. Example: Rxd8+ deflecting the back-rank defender. Frequency: decoy and deflection in roughly 5-10% of decisive club tactics; windmill and desperado rare. Verb: recognize.; Defensive resources for the side that is worse: stalemate tricks (stalemateWatch.ts:24-40 only warns the WINNING student), perpetual-check resource, fortress, and a repetition claim. Nothing in conceptEngine, positionFacts or liveTacticsContext detects 'you are lost, but there is a perpetual or stalemate here'. Example: queen sac to stalemate with K+P vs K+Q. Frequency: about 1-3% of games. Verbs: identify / strategize.; Avoid repetition when winning, and the fifty-move rule. No position-level computer tracks threefold repetition or the move count for the student. Example: a winning student repeating checks. Frequency: rare at club level, decisive when it happens. Verb: prevent.; Réti two-goal king: a king walking a diagonal chases two goals at once (the brief's golden nugget). No computer exists (grep for 'two goals', 'Réti', 'diagonal' is empty in services). Example: the Réti study, K h8 vs pawn races. Frequency: rare, but taught in every endgame course. Verb: knowledge.; Insufficient material and draw-by-trade awareness during the middlegame trade-down: 'don't trade your last pawn, K+N vs K is a draw'; wrong-coloured bishop + rook pawn as a trade CONSEQUENCE. conceptEngine covers mating material only against a bare king. detectRookPawnCorner exists, but only reaches Learn through the max:1 concept slot. Verb: consequence.; Knight geometry: how many moves a knight needs to reach a square, that a knight cannot lose a tempo (it changes square colour every move), and knight vs rook pawn. knightReach exists only as an internal helper (moveInsight / speedRunReads) and is never taught as a fact. Verb: knowledge.; Pawn breakthrough combinations: the classic three-vs-three sac on the fifth rank, and the 'two connected passers on the sixth beat a rook' type of count. Only the inline gambitLine (CoachTeachPage.tsx:10705-10751) notices a fresh passer after a sac; nothing computes the breakthrough before it happens. Verbs: recognize / plan.; Exchange sacrifice (rook for minor piece) taught live as a positional idea: what it buys (structure, a dark-square bind). It exists only in reviewSacrifice and never reaches Learn. Frequency: about 1% of master games. Verb: strategize.; Underpromotion necessity: a knight promotion with check or fork, or a rook promotion to avoid stalemate. No live computer exists. Rare. Verb: knowledge.; Opponent tendencies across games (F18 step 5 is in-game only through thinkAloud.opponentHabits). Learn's opponent is the coach, so this is n/a for Learn. It is noted for Review and imported games.
BROKEN: GEM NEVER REACHES A LEARN STUDENT ON A NAMED OPENING (David's acceptance test). In resolveCoachReplyMove, the book move of the named opening returns at CoachTeachPage.tsx:7644-7652, before getAdaptiveMove at 7687. pickTaughtSlip (coachGameEngine.ts:417), and so the gem slip, is never consulted while the student is on the opening they asked for. Off book, slipsAllowed (coachGameEngine.ts:239-249) blocks every slip for studentElo >= 1000 on Medium/auto, blocks all of them on Hard and above 2000, and allows at most one per game (432-440). A student around 1300 on the default setting can never be handed a gem. The rating is deciding teaching here (F10).; Gem callout lane carries no squares (CoachTeachPage.tsx:8846), so nothing is highlighted when it speaks. gemResolution speaks through speakComputed outside the door (9098) and is never added to spokenKeys.; Post-move grade speaks outside decideTurn via speakComputed (CoachTeachPage.tsx:9271-9278). This violates D1 (one decider), and the line opens with bare SAN ('Nf3: …').; One student move gets three costs. gradePlayedMove reads it from the fan (9191, which feeds studentCpLoss → positionFacts). liveMoveCost runs one search (10449, which feeds backwardLook and the lanes). useDiscussionPractice takes the delta of two fresh reads (useDiscussionPractice.ts:400-423, which feeds captureMisconception, capability evidence, liveStrength and the hint dial). The page's own comment at 10445-10447 rejects that two-read method. What is spoken and what is recorded can disagree (G7, D7, F13).; The coach's move cost is computed twice, identically: liveMoveCost at CoachTeachPage.tsx:10983 (oppLoss) and again at 11002.; theirIntent re-reads fenAfterReply (CoachTeachPage.tsx:10926) after the turn already read that same FEN with MultiPV 6 (9425). Because the cache key ignores MultiPV (census C), the two reads can collide.; noteTaughtThisTurn is dead. It is only set when the instant package keeps a kind 'note' (10309-10311), and no instant lane has kind 'note' (gem, tactic, threat, opening, openingIdea). The stand-down branch at 11106-11108 never fires.; must-defend is computed and then dropped. positionFacts produces it, and Learn skips it at CoachTeachPage.tsx:9773 because its own inline threat alert uses a different hanging/threat definition. This is the G8.5 'computed and dropped' class.; Hard caps (V14): REJECTED_TEMPTING_MAX_PER_GAME = 2 (CoachTeachPage.tsx:220, 9852), the .slice(0,4) on rejected-tempting lines (9854), conceptForBoard max: 1 (positionFacts.ts:803, only one concept per position can speak), and threat squares scraped from prose then .slice(0,4) (CoachTeachPage.tsx:8245).; Arrows are re-derived from prose. namedMoveArrows scans every queued sentence (CoachTeachPage.tsx:9024-9031, learnBoardTeaching.ts:432). This is exactly the root cause in brief item 4.; Lanes that speak with no squares at all: phase (7801), structure (9592), character (9920), theirMoveCost verdict (10987), coach look (11057), opening (8453), and strongChoice, blunderCheck, keepPressing, pushOrHold and planChoice (all squares: []). Lanes with squares but no arrow for the move or line they describe: countMethod (no attacker/defender arrows), checkMethod, stalemate (the stalemating move), tempo, kneeJerk, planArc, openingIdentity.; Learn says 'I' (V1/V2). opponentGap 'coach-is-opponent' produces 'I let you off there' (opponentGap.ts:88), selected at CoachTeachPage.tsx:9547. The hintRegister doc examples are also first person (hintRegister.ts:20-22).; Numbers in speech (V8): the trapAhead text '${t.freqPct}% of club players play it' (learnBoardTeaching.ts:563) and gemCrushLines.ts:786 '${share}% of club players'. 'Mate in N' counts are also spoken (CoachTeachPage.tsx:8101-8104).; Rating decides what is said (F10/F15/F16). The page reads rating = puzzleRating ?? currentRating (CoachTeachPage.tsx:7989, 9406, 10030; also 2786, 2980). It feeds the buildTacticsLiveContext look-ahead depth (which threats are found), positionFacts and conceptForBoard, and causalChainVoice.depthFor(rating), which sets how much of the chain is spoken (causalChainVoice.ts:47-49). isBeginnerMode still feeds decideTurn's BEGINNER_ALWAYS (learnTurnDoor.ts:316, CoachTeachPage.tsx:7801/8879/11138). The hint dial is seeded by coreRatingTier (hintRegister.ts:28).; The legacy weakness reader getStoredWeaknessProfile is still called at CoachTeachPage.tsx:11587, beside the spine (R4; census D B1).; The F18 thinking chain (thinkAloud.depthClauses + speedRunReads) reaches Learn only as clauses inside the single 'positionFacts' lane (lead 50, positionFacts.ts:1009). About 50 other lanes are offered next to it and ordered by stake and DNA beat, so there is no single goal/obstacle/remove-it chain (F0c not met).; strongChoice says 'Strong players play X here', but the data comes from one player's game DB (strongChoice.ts:9-52, hisPlayLookup). That is a generalization the data does not support (D10).; The live tactic vocabulary (TacticPatternType, types/tacticTypes.ts:39-51) is narrower than TacticType (types/index.ts:1700-1722). The instant alert can only name fork, pin, skewer, discovery, double check, back rank, removal of guard, trapped piece, mate threat, overload and battery. Deflection, clearance, interference, zwischenzug and x-ray exist only for after-the-fact classification (R5).
COMPUTERS: decideTurn + LEARN_LANES / DNA_BEAT / DESCRIPTION_LEAD (the Learn door) (src/services/learnTurnDoor.ts:138-261, 319-447) — Decides which offered lane facts speak and in what order. Danger comes first, then stakes (factStakes), then the DNA beat. A description (lead <=40) speaks only as support of the lead. Asides under one pawn at stake are held. Green tags fade to the first sentence. Used 3 times on one turn: phase at CoachTeachPage:7801, instant at 8845, late at 11120. The callout names the opportunity and holds back the move. The opponent plays the slip deliberately, at most once per game. ## Review surface end to end (CoachGameReview.tsx 4,522 lines, coachFeatureService.ts 5,429 lines, reviewFullData.ts 1,351 lines + the review* services they call)
Review is the richest computer stack in the app, and most of it reaches the student. It is also the clearest case of F0c being broken. Each ply's teaching is a bag of tagged STRINGS: computeMoveFacets (reviewFullData.ts:242) pushes about 40 facet kinds, such as `[tactic]`, `[trade]`, `[refuted]` and `[plan-now]`. decide() (coachFeatureService.ts:2308) then ranks, subsumes and filters those strings and buildVoicePackage joins them. Nothing assigns a facet a ROLE (goal/reason/obstacle/remove-it/tempting/line/habit). So a ply reads as a stack of findings, not one thought, and F18's order (what changed, then tempting choices, then "not yet, first this", then the line, then the habit) is never built. Squares are attached to only part of the facets (recSquares, 27 calls), and only as a side map keyed by the facet TEXT. The board arrows for named moves are then pulled back OUT of the finished sentence by segmentNamedArrows→namedMoveArrows (coachFeatureService.ts:611, learnBoardTeaching.ts:432), which is exactly the S10 disease.

About 860 lines of the old one-beat "capped cascade" (coachFeatureService.ts:2532-3395) are dead in prod, because isReviewUncapped() defaults to true (reviewNarrationBuild.ts:28-35). They are dead duplicates of the facet producers, and they include one of the two gem-crush call sites. Four turning-point or critical-moment pickers and five recap producers run on the same page. Two of the three question cards still record nothing to capabilityEvidence. For the one brain: Review supplies the facts the chain needs (refutation, the line, cause counted across the game, the habit through decide/methodBeat), but its facts must be given roles and carry their squares and moves. The dead cascade, the dead cards and the prose→arrow step should go.
MISSING: Clock and time management: no review computer reads PGN %clk (zero review-path hits). The coach cannot say 'you spent 4 minutes on a forced recapture, then blitzed the critical move'. detectBadHabitsFromGame guesses time pressure from 'the last 10 moves' (coachFeatureService.ts:237). F01: PLAN/habit. Frequency: every timed import.; Endgame technique in review: opposition, key squares, square of the pawn, triangulation, zugzwang, Lucena/Philidor, wrong bishop + rook pawn, insufficient material and stalemate resources. All exist in conceptEngine/endgameTechnique, but review imports only tacticInvariant/definitionKey (reviewFullData.ts:25). An endgame ply gets nameEndgamePhase and readConversion only. F01: KNOWLEDGE. Example: K+P ending where the student lost opposition. ~1 in 5 games reach an endgame.; Missed defensive resource in a losing position: perpetual check, stalemate trick, fortress, the most testing practical try or swindle. Zero 'swindle' hits, and perpetual/fortress are absent from review. F01: PREVENT/STRATEGIZE. Example: down a rook with a perpetual available, student plays on and loses.; Zwischenzug / intermezzo missed or allowed in a recapture sequence. No review-path hits. F01: RECOGNIZE. Example: student auto-recaptures when a check first wins a tempo. Common in amateur games.; X-ray / skewer-through on review plies: no review-path hits (it exists in tacticsDetector, but no review facet names it). F01: RECOGNIZE.; Own king left uncastled / too long in the centre: enemyKingStuckInCenter covers only the OPPONENT (reviewSacrifice.ts:113). There is no symmetric 'your king has been in the centre for N moves with the e-file opening'. F01: PREVENT.; Tempo/development count over the opening, said as arithmetic ('you moved the queen three times; they developed three pieces'). Counted per principle, but there is no running tempo ledger surfaced as a number-free comparison. F01: KNOWLEDGE (golden nugget).; Repeated opponent mistake pattern (F18 step 5, 'what the opponent keeps doing wrong'): the cause is counted for the STUDENT (turningCause), but nothing counts the opponent's recurring error across the game for the student to exploit. F01: STRATEGIZE.; Threefold repetition / fifty-move draw claimed or missed while winning or losing. 'fifty' has 0 review hits. F01: KNOWLEDGE. Rare, maybe 1 in 200 games.; Underpromotion and en passant opportunities missed: zero review-path hits. F01: KNOWLEDGE. Rare.; Principle of two weaknesses / switching wings in a won ending: 0 review hits. F01: STRATEGIZE.; Opposite-coloured bishops drawing tendency when trading into one: only the opening orientation mentions it (reviewStrategicOrientation). There is no trade-down judgement. F01: CONSEQUENCE.; Candidate-move discipline: the moves a player at this level would consider at a turning point, each ruled out with its refutation (F18 step 2). refutedAlternative covers only the single most-played human move, and only in opening/explorer positions. Middlegame turning points have no tempting-choice set. F01: CONSEQUENCE.; Master-game reference by structure fingerprint (brief item 2): pickStoryGame matches only by opening name (reviewStoryGame.ts:134). Nothing matches by pawn structure, material or tactic just played. F01: KNOWLEDGE.; Gem slip the STUDENT played (they fell into a known trap) as a review teaching beat. computeGemCrush is called only with opponentSide logic for 'avoided' or 'opponent played' (reviewFullData.ts:994-1006). The student's own walk-into of a mined trap is not named as a trap with its frequency. F01: PREVENT. Directly on David's opening priority (F02-F04).
BROKEN: The capped cascade (~860 lines) is DEAD in production: coachFeatureService.ts:2532-3395 runs only when uncapped=false, and isReviewUncapped() returns true by default (reviewNarrationBuild.ts:28-35). The uncapped branch `continue`s at :2530. Dead with it: the gem-crush review note (:2955-2975, which the comment at reviewFullData.ts:985-986 admits), buildReviewDeepestLookahead :2866, buildMissedShotSignal :2628, describeSimplifyingTrade :2885, describeTradeConsequence :2894, describeNotableMove :3206, the second assessPositionalEdge :3073, and the threat/prevention lanes :2834-2933.; S10: arrows for named moves are re-derived from the finished prose. segmentNamedArrows (coachFeatureService.ts:611) calls namedMoveArrows (learnBoardTeaching.ts:432), which regex-parses the narration. Facet squares exist only as a side map keyed by facet TEXT (reviewFullData.ts:280). Many facets record no squares at all: [move], [eval], [verdict], [structure], [king], [count], [royal], [rook7], [badbishop], [worst], [plan-now], [plan-race], [sac], [stock], [opening], [opp-dev], [endgame].; F0c/F18: facets are untyped tagged strings (`[tag] prose`). No role (goal/reason/obstacle/remove-it/tempting/line/habit) exists anywhere in the review path, so the chain cannot be composed. buildVoicePackage just joins the kept parts (coachFeatureService.ts:2486-2500).; Cap left behind: selectTurningPoints slices to TURNING_POINTS_ASKED=3 (turningPoints.ts:28, :273). Meanwhile CoachGameReview.tsx:1197 says 'REVIEW_QUESTION_BUDGET is Infinity'. That constant no longer exists. The comment is false and the cap is a G4.5 violation.; useReviewBlunderCapture is an inert stub (hooks/useReviewBlunderCapture.ts:~50, phase always 'idle'). Its whole render block, review-blunder-capture (CoachGameReview.tsx:4197-4260), is dead UI.; The why-picker faucet is dormant: useDiscussionPractice is mounted (CoachGameReview.tsx:947-958), but raiseSlipPrompt is never destructured.
